### Decision 2026-08-05-075: Operational invariant RATIFIED — contribution pools are single-calendar-month instruments; cross-month/cross-year pools require an explicit Trustee Panel emergency resolution

**Decision type:** Other — governance ratification of an operational invariant surfaced during code review
**Status:** Trustee-ratified (BigDev)
**Author:** BigDev
**Ratifying trustees:** BigDev

**Context:**
Code review of Story 10.24 (2026-08-05) surfaced a candidate edge case in `skips_current_year`'s
derivation (`packages/domain/src/contribution/facts.ts`, `missedCycleAggregateSql`/`istYearStartUtc`):
a pool cycle assigned in December but closed in January would bucket by assignment year (per AC4's
literal text — "assignments in the IST calendar year of `at`"), and so would fall into neither
calendar year's skip count under a naive read of the derivation as written.

BigDev's ruling: this is not a gap in the derivation. It is a **precondition the derivation is entitled
to assume**, because the governance model does not admit cycles that straddle a calendar-month
boundary in normal operation — assignment-year and close-year are identical by construction. A cycle
that spans months or years is outside the supported operating model and would require an extraordinary,
explicitly Trustee-declared exception, not an alternative code path in the fact producer.

**Decision:**
1. **Operational invariant, binding on all pool/cycle-scoped derivations, not just Story 10.24:**
   Contribution pools are single-calendar-month instruments. A pool cycle MUST open and close within
   the same calendar month (and therefore the same calendar year). `skips_current_year`'s
   assignment-year bucketing is correct as shipped under this invariant; no code change to Story 10.24
   is required.
2. Cross-month or cross-calendar-year pool cycles are **not part of normal system behaviour**. They
   may occur only under an explicit Trustee Panel emergency resolution (e.g., a force-majeure
   extension), and any such resolution must separately consider whether it invalidates the
   assignment-year bucketing assumption in `skips_current_year` (and any other derivation that assumes
   single-month cycles) for the affected cycle.
3. This invariant is recorded here, rather than only in Story 10.24's file, because it constrains every
   future pool/cycle-scoped derivation across the codebase (Epic 7 pool substrate, Epic 8/9 contribution
   producers, Epic 10 trustee/moderation surfaces), not only the one that surfaced it.

**Open follow-ups:**
- No code change required for Story 10.24 (`skips_current_year` ships as-is).
- Future pool/cycle-scoped derivations should cite this invariant rather than re-deriving or
  re-litigating the single-calendar-month assumption.
- If a Trustee Panel emergency resolution ever produces a cross-month/cross-year cycle, revisit
  `skips_current_year` and any sibling derivation before that cycle is evaluated for R7 purposes.

**References:**
- `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/10-24-contribution-fact-producer-projection-r7-cf-activation.md` — Story 10.24, AC4, Review Findings section (code review, 2026-08-05)
- `packages/domain/src/contribution/facts.ts` — `missedCycleAggregateSql`/`istYearStartUtc`

### Decision 2026-08-05-074: `missed-closed-cycle-v1` RATIFIED as the versioned implementation policy for `contribution.in_lapse`; the Story 10.16 restoration-package count CONFIRMED as Story 10.25's

**Decision type:** Other — governance ratification of implementation-policy escalations
**Status:** Trustee-ratified (BigDev)
**Author:** BigDev
**Ratifying trustees:** BigDev

**Context:**
Decision 2026-08-05-073 (Story 10.24) was author-committed with five escalations, two of which were
addressed to BigDev as decisions the implementation could not make for itself:

- **Escalation 1** — `contribution.in_lapse` shipped under a NAMED derivation policy
  (`missed-closed-cycle-v1`) because "lapse" has no Niyamavali-pinned definition and 10.24's boundary
  forbade defining governance policy. The story argued the ratification window was TIME-SENSITIVE: no
  ACTIVATED clause reads `in_lapse` today (only the HELD R7(A)), so a re-pin was cheap in eligibility
  terms — but the fact was already on the wire, hashed into `validityPayloadHash`, and rendered as
  `holdingSince` on a suspension surface.
- **Escalation 3** — 10.24 re-pointed Story 10.16's `restorationPackage` producer label from
  `story-10-24` to `story-10-25`, on the grounds that the consecutive-contribution count is RESTORATION
  ACCOUNTING (outside 10.24's boundary, and coupled to Story 10.23's separately-expiring overlay). The
  story flagged that ruling otherwise would be a SCOPE CHANGE, revertible in one literal.

**Decision:**

1. **`missed-closed-cycle-v1` is CONFIRMED as the ratified versioned implementation policy for
   `contribution.in_lapse`.** Rationale as given: `contribution.in_lapse` is now part of the validity
   payload contract; no activated clause currently depends on it, which made this the LOWEST-COST point
   to ratify the policy.

2. **Future changes to this policy, once it is consumed by member ELIGIBILITY rules, are to be treated
   as GOVERNANCE changes rather than implementation refinements.** This raises the bar for change rather
   than lowering it: the cheap-re-pin window that existed while the policy was un-ratified is now
   CLOSED. A re-pin requires a new decision-log entry SUPERSEDING this one — never a refactor, and never
   an "it was only v1" edit. The blast radius is unchanged and remains migration-shaped: every payload
   hash moves, every cached row is re-shaped, every recorded flag's onset can shift.
   *Recorded in code at the definition site* (`packages/validity-service/src/producer.ts`,
   `ContributionLapsePolicy`) and pinned by a test that states why the literal is not free to move
   (`tests/contribution-facts.test.ts`).

3. **Story 10.25 is CONFIRMED as the owner of the Story 10.16 restoration-package count.** The
   `story-10-24` → `story-10-25` label re-point stands. This is no longer an open scope question; moving
   it again needs a superseding decision, not a judgement call.

**Open follow-ups:**

- Escalation 1 is RESOLVED — closed by ratification, not by deferral
  ([[feedback_closure_language_precision]]). Escalation 3 is RESOLVED — confirmed as proposed.
- Story 10.24's remaining escalations are unaffected and stay recorded where they were: **Escalation 2**
  (the fully-provisioned p95 delta is un-attested — `deferred-work.md`), **Escalation 4** (the FR-12A
  documented `contribution_history` shape variance + `missed_count_lifetime` not supplied — recorded as
  a variance, NOT invented), **Escalation 5** (stale line anchors in the change proposal; every anchor
  was re-verified live before use).
- When Story 10.25 lands and `contribution.r7a_restorations_used` is supplied, R7(A) becomes activatable
  and will READ `in_lapse` — that is the moment clause 2 above starts to bite. The
  `R7_HELD_CLAUSES` totality test fires at exactly that point, which is the intended trigger.

**References:**
- Decision 2026-08-05-073 (Story 10.24 implementation record — D5 and D7 are the subjects here)
- `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/10-24-contribution-fact-producer-projection-r7-cf-activation.md`
  (Escalations 1 and 3)
- `packages/validity-service/src/producer.ts` (`ContributionLapsePolicy` / `CONTRIBUTION_LAPSE_POLICY`)
- `packages/ui/src/contribution-disclosure/{view-model,presenter}.ts` (the 10.16 label)

### Decision 2026-08-05-073: Story 10.24 — the contribution-fact producer lands: two projection mechanisms held observationally equivalent, APPLIED-ONLY clause contribution, a versioned lapse policy, and the 10.16 label re-pointed to 10.25

**Decision type:** Other — implementation-substrate decision record (solo-builder author-committed)
**Status:** Solo-builder-author-committed
**Author:** Amelia (dev agent), for BigDev
**Ratifying trustees:** pending (Escalations 1 and 3 below are addressed TO the Trustee Panel / BigDev)

**Context:**
Story 4.2 deferred the `contribution.*` FACT producer to "Epic 8/9". Both epics closed `done` with
retrospectives and nothing was built, because two producers were conflated — the `contribution.confirmed`
EVENT producer WAS built (Story 9.4, two live emitters), but nothing ever mapped those events onto the
seven `contribution.*` keys the R7 ladder reads — and because the deferral named an EPIC, and epics carry
no acceptance criteria, so no story owned it. Consequence, live until this story: no R7 violation could be
detected and no member's completion of a restoration package could be observed by any surface.
[[project_r7_fact_producer_unbuilt]]

**Decision:**

1. **D2 — only APPLIED R7 clauses enter `applicableNiyamavaliClauses[]`.** `assembleClauses` pushes every
   non-null slot, and `deriveViolatorFlags` maps every R7 clause id it finds into a violator flag with NO
   `applied` check. Wiring the four activated clauses as ordinary `RuleDescriptor`s would therefore flag
   EVERY member in the Pariwar, four times each, with outcome `r7_not_applicable` — a governance surface
   recommending suspension of everyone. The family is instead evaluated through `evaluateLadderAt` (the
   generic ladder, parameterized by `R7_ACTIVATED_CLAUSE_IDS` — never the `evaluateR7Ladder*` wrappers,
   which hardcode all seven and would silently evaluate the HELD three), and only
   `perClauseResults.filter(e => e.applied)` is contributed. `applied` is computed from the payload's own
   `on_pass` DATA by the ladder's `isApplied`, with its swapped-payload guard; it is NOT re-derived as
   `decision !== 'r7_not_applicable'`, which would be a second definition minus the guard. EVERY applied
   clause is contributed, not just the ladder's precedence pick — `precedence` orders which explanation
   surfaces, never eligibility ([[project_niyamavali_precedence_is_provenance]]).
   *Revert-probe run and recorded:* removing the `.filter` turned a clean member's payload from zero R7
   entries into FOUR. Restored.

2. **D4 — `VALIDITY_RULE_ORDER` is the omission mechanism, and the hold is MECHANIZED.** An omitted clause
   never gets a descriptor: not evaluated, not memoized, not audited, and structurally unable to appear in
   `applicableNiyamavaliClauses[]` or `provenanceTrace[]` — strictly stronger than evaluate-then-filter.
   R7(A)/(B)/(G) are held under `R7_HELD_CLAUSES`, each naming its blocking fact and owner. A totality test
   asserts `activated ∪ held === R7_CLAUSE_IDS` with an EMPTY intersection, that no held id and no R8 id is
   in `VALIDITY_RULE_ORDER`, and that every `blockedBy` key is genuinely absent from
   `R7_SUPPLIED_FACT_KEYS` — so a hold cannot outlive its reason.
   **R7(A)/(B) are held even though `total_count` and `ever_contributed` ARE supplied**, because
   `prd.md:346` makes evaluating them from those proxies NORMATIVELY forbidden. Supplying a fact and
   activating a clause are different acts.

3. **⭐ D3 — TWO projection mechanisms, ONE contract, with the equivalence obligation ATTACHED.**
   `member_contribution_ledger` is maintained by an `events_log` AFTER-INSERT trigger (atomic with the
   append; covers any future writer, including the backfill and any repair path; cannot be forgotten —
   which matters more here than elsewhere, since the failure being fixed is "a producer nobody owned").
   `member_pool_assignments` is maintained by an EXPLICIT writer in `pool/spawn.ts`, because a trigger on
   `pool_snapshots` would expand a JSONB array of up to 4L/N member ids inside the spawn transaction,
   un-instrumented, inside Story 7.9's <60s envelope.
   **THE MECHANISM IS AN IMPLEMENTATION DETAIL; THE PROJECTED STATE IS THE CONTRACT.** The difference
   between them must be WHERE the write is invoked and nothing else. Both are held to atomicity ·
   idempotency · replay equivalence · ordering-independence by ONE SHARED TEST BODY run twice (never two
   parallel files that can drift), plus the acceptance-level check that `deriveContributionFacts` over the
   incrementally-maintained tables equals it over freshly-backfilled tables for the same `(member, at)`.
   **A future author adding a THIRD projection inherits this obligation, not merely the precedent.**

4. **D5 — `missed-closed-cycle-v1` is a VERSIONED payload-contract element, NOT a placeholder.**
   "Lapse" has no Niyamavali-pinned definition, and this story's boundary forbids defining governance
   policy — but the epic AC names `in_lapse` among the five facts, so it ships under a named, documented
   derivation, exactly as `valid_membership_years` ships under `LapseNettingPolicy` ([[CR-4.5-D2]]).
   **Read "v1" as a VERSION, not an expiry date.** The moment it shipped it became part of the
   `contributionHistorySummary` payload contract: hashed into `validityPayloadHash`, read by trustee-lite
   `factsEstablishing[]`, rendered as `holdingSince` on a suspension surface. Re-pinning it later is a
   contract change with a migration-shaped blast radius — reviewed and versioned like any other, never an
   "it was only v1, so I retuned it" edit.
   *Rule:* in lapse iff ≥1 assigned-and-closed cycle in the current IST calendar year resolved without a
   live confirmation; `lapseSince` = the close instant of the EARLIEST such cycle.

5. **D7 — Story 10.16's restoration-package label re-points `story-10-24` → `story-10-25`.**
   `RestorationPackageState.ok` needs `{remaining, required}` — the count of CONSECUTIVE contributions
   against `restoration.consecutive_required`. That is RESTORATION ACCOUNTING, which this story's boundary
   excludes and which couples to Story 10.23's restoration-discipline overlay with SEPARATE expiry (the
   non-subsumption principle, [[project_moderation_model_correct_course]]). Building it here would collapse
   two independently amendable governance instruments into one release. Leaving the label at `story-10-24`
   after this story shipped would have turned an honest sentinel into a lie — it would name a story that
   has shipped and did not close the gap. One-literal change either way.

6. **The Story 7.4 assignment version pin is NOT bumped, and that is PROVEN, not asserted.**
   `POOL_ASSIGNMENT_HASH_VERSION` gates the ALGORITHM (hash fn, truncation width, delimiter, balancing
   rule), not the roster's contents; the roster reads `payload.isAssignable` and nothing else, and
   `deriveIsAssignable` is a function of lifecycle state + moderation status only — contribution facts
   cannot move it. A bump would break replay of every already-frozen cycle (10.17 D3, ratified). Evidenced
   by a re-spawn test asserting a byte-identical roster hash and byte-identical assignments after
   confirmation + reversal events land.

**Open follow-ups:**

- **Escalation 1 — the `in_lapse` derivation policy (D5).** BigDev / the Trustee Panel are asked to CONFIRM
  or RE-PIN `missed-closed-cycle-v1` **now, while the window is cheap**: no ACTIVATED clause reads
  `in_lapse` today (only the HELD R7(A)), so a re-pin costs nothing in eligibility terms. That bounds the
  GOVERNANCE cost of a re-pin; it does NOT make the fact disposable — it is already on the wire, hashed
  into `validityPayloadHash`, and rendered as `holdingSince` on the trustee moderation surface. Once a
  clause starts reading it, re-pinning becomes a governance change rather than a derivation change.
- **Escalation 3 — who owns the restoration-package count?** This story re-points 10.16's label to 10.25.
  Confirm 10.25 is the right owner, or rule that 10.24 should have supplied it — a SCOPE CHANGE either way,
  and a one-literal revert.
- **Escalation 2 — measured cost.** No material p95 movement observed (uncached p50 +0.6–0.8 ms; p95 within
  run-to-run noise), and the binding structural gate — no new N+1 query path — passes with a counted-query
  assertion. The FULLY-PROVISIONED delta is un-attested; see `deferred-work.md`.
- **Escalation 4 — FR-12A variance.** The documented `contribution_history` shape (`prd.md:404-406`) is not
  what ships, and `missed_count_lifetime` is not supplied. Recorded as a variance in `deferred-work.md`; a
  lifetime miss count was NOT invented.
- **Escalation 5 — stale anchors.** The change proposal's `payload.ts:294` citation was stale at `55aa1cc`
  (`:283-286`, use at `:336`). Every anchor cited in the story was re-verified live before use
  ([[feedback_verify_before_committing_governance_claims]]).

**References:**
- `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/10-24-contribution-fact-producer-projection-r7-cf-activation.md`
- `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/sprint-change-proposal-2026-08-04-R2.md` — the origin document
- `prd.md:339-356` (FR-9 + the normative no-proxy consequence at `:346`); `:385-433` (FR-12A)
- Migration `0093_contribution-fact-projection.sql`; `packages/validity-service/src/rules.ts` (D2/D4);
  `packages/validity-service/src/producer.ts` (D5); `packages/domain/src/contribution/{facts,projection-write}.ts`
- Decision 2026-08-04-072 (moderation-model correct-course recording scope); Story 10.17 D3/D5; Story 10.11 D1-B

### Decision 2026-08-04-072: Architecture-recording scope for the moderation-model correct-course (§4f) — canonical record is the AI-series invariant comments in Stories 10.17/10.19/10.23, not `architecture.md`

**Decision type:** Governance — Sprint Change Proposal architecture-recording scope.
**Status:** Solo-builder-author-committed.
**Author:** BigDev (via `bmad-architecture`, Update intent, invoked to apply `sprint-change-proposal-2026-08-04.md` §4f).
**Ratifying trustees:** BigDev (process/documentation-convention decision, not a funds or legal matter — no ≥2-trustee quorum sought).

**Context:**
`sprint-change-proposal-2026-08-04.md` §4f assigns three architecture items to the moderation-model correct-course: (a) `MemberValidityPayload` gains `is_assignable` as a pre-derived field, (b) the restoration-discipline overlay's states/transitions/events (Story 10.23), and (c) the auth-path overlay read for termination (Story 10.19) — plus recording the amendment to the frozen **AI-7-2** invariant. Investigating where these belong found: `architecture.md` (the formal spine) was frozen at Step 8, `completedAt: 2026-05-26` — before Epic 4 existed — and contains zero references to `MemberValidityPayload`, `is_valid`, overlays, or any `AI-N-M` invariant. No `.memlog.md` exists anywhere in the project (the memlog/spine machinery this skill normally uses was never adopted here). The project's actual, established convention since roughly Epic 4 is to record epic-level architectural invariants as a structured doc-comment at the point of use in source, tagged `AI-{epic}-{n}` (confirmed at `apps/jobs/src/assignable-roster.ts:1-53`, which documents AI-7-2 in full), cross-referenced by memory notes rather than by a central architecture document.

**Decision:**
1. The three §4f architecture items are **ratified by the Sprint Change Proposal** — no further design elicitation is required before Stories 10.17, 10.19, and 10.23 implement them.
2. Their **canonical implementation record SHALL be the `AI`-series invariant comments** introduced by Stories 10.17 (the `is_assignable` predicate + the AI-7-2 amendment, at `assignable-roster.ts` and `payload.ts`), 10.19 (the auth-path overlay-read invariant), and 10.23 (the restoration-discipline overlay invariant) at the point each is implemented — following the AI-7-2 precedent.
3. **No `architecture.md` amendment is performed** as part of this correct-course, and none is required going forward under this convention — unless the project explicitly re-adopts `architecture.md` as the active architectural spine, in which case this decision should be revisited.
4. This `bmad-architecture` run therefore closes with **no spine artifact produced or updated**; this decision-log entry is the durable record that the scoping question was raised and answered, so a future correct-course cycle does not re-discover `architecture.md` as stale and re-litigate the same question.

**Open follow-ups:**
- ~~Story 10.17 dev-story: write the `is_assignable` predicate and amend the AI-7-2 doc block at `assignable-roster.ts:41-53` + `payload.ts:77,290` + `types.ts:167` + `contracts/src/members/validity.ts:111`; update memory `[[project_assignability_predicate_is_isvalid_only]]` at that time (flagged there now as "amendment ratified, implementation pending Story 10.17").~~ — **RESOLVED 2026-08-04 by Story 10.17** (branch `feat/10-17-moderation-roster-unblock`). `deriveIsAssignable(state, moderationStatus) = VALID_STATES.includes(state) && moderationStatus !== 'terminated'` now sits beside `deriveIsValid` in `packages/validity-service/src/payload.ts`, is wired into `assemblePayload`, and is exported from the package index; `isAssignable` was added to `MemberValidityPayload` (`types.ts`) and to the `.strict()` `MemberValidityPayloadDto` (`contracts/src/members/validity.ts`). `apps/jobs/src/assignable-roster.ts` now returns `payload.isAssignable` and its doc block was rewritten as **the canonical amendment record** for AI-7-2 per point 2 of this decision — naming what changed (the single field read), what did NOT (still exactly ONE pre-derived field; any other subfield read on that path is still a review finding), and why (Niyamavali §3.3 — suspension removes the entitlement to RECEIVE support, not the obligation to CONTRIBUTE). The wrong enforcement-scope claim at `payload.ts` (Story 10.10's Decision-8 comment, which asserted claim eligibility and the rules engine also inherited suspension through `is_valid` — only pool assignability ever did) was corrected in the same pass, as was its duplicate at `packages/domain/src/member/moderation/index.ts`. Per point 3, **no `architecture.md` amendment was performed.** Memory `[[project_assignability_predicate_is_isvalid_only]]` updated to `shipped`.
  - ⚠ **One sub-item of Story 10.17's own AC3 could NOT be discharged and is carried as deferred work, not silently dropped:** `openapi/v1.yaml` regenerated to a **byte-identical file**, because `packages/contracts/scripts/emit-openapi.ts` is a hand-curated registry that has never imported `../src/members/validity.js` — the Story 4.6 validity payload has never been in the OpenAPI artifact at all (`grep -c isValid openapi/v1.yaml` → 0). The two-boolean prose was written into the `MemberValidityPayloadDto` doc comment instead. Recorded in `deferred-work.md` under *"Deferred from: implementation of 10-17-moderation-roster-unblock (2026-08-04)"*.
- Story 10.19 dev-story: introduce the auth-path overlay-read invariant at `member-auth.handlers.ts:71` with its own `AI-10-n` comment block.
- Story 10.23 dev-story: introduce the restoration-discipline overlay invariant (D2-a, a second overlay mirroring the shipped moderation overlay) with its own `AI-10-n` comment block.
- If `architecture.md` is ever re-adopted as the living spine, backfill Epic 4–10's `AI-*` invariants into it as a dedicated migration effort, not piecemeal.

**References:**

### Decision 2026-08-01-071: Trustee Panel ratifies ADR-0032, ADR-0033, ADR-0034 (bank-statement portion), and ADR-0035 — the bank-statement / reconciliation ADR batch

**Decision type:** Governance — ADR ratification (Trustee Panel consent-sheet session).
**Status:** Trustee-ratified.
**Author:** Solo Builder (session facilitation)
**Ratifying trustees:** Dhiraj Rahul (Trustee 1) + Kalpana Bharti (Trustee 2) — ≥2-trustee quorum met, 2026-08-01.

**Context:**
ADR-0032 (bank-statement normalization schema), ADR-0033 (bank-statement intake pipeline), ADR-0034 (object-storage tier policy, bank-statement portion), and ADR-0035 (reconciliation matcher mechanism) were authored 2026-07-26 at Story 9.2/9.3/9.4 closure, each carrying a header citing "Trustee ratification is Story 14.7's AR-69 backlog closure." Story 14.7's actual acceptance criteria (`epics.md:4410-4431`) govern five *vendor-selection-pending* capability bars and do not name these four — the header's citation was a placeholder for "ratify later, in a batch" (the ADR-0026 precedent), not a hard gate. Following the same session that ratified ADR-0036 earlier the same day, this batch was presented via `docs/knowledge-transfer/adr-ratification-consent-sheet-2026-08-01-bank-statement-batch.md`.

Ahead of presentation, the Trustee Panel requested and reviewed four content revisions, applied directly to the still-`drafted` ADR files (ordinary pre-ratification authoring, not an amendment to a ratified record):

1. **ADR-0035** gained a **"Governance principles"** section: (a) *Decision-support scope* — states plainly that automatic confirmation is limited to a deterministic, mathematically-unambiguous exact match (pool + UTR + amount); any anomaly exits the automatic path and requires human adjudication through the shipped Story 9.8 review queue (confirm/reject/facilitate-recovery/review-and-reverse, each step-up-OTP + reason-code + audit-gated); a stricter posture (human sign-off on every confirmation, including exact matches) is named as a future, trustee-invocable change to the operating model, not a claim about what ships today. (b) *Recovery, not automatic reversal* — states that TWT cannot automatically debit or recover funds from a nominee or any party, citing the Story 7.6 pool-bound-payment CI gate and the Story 9.8 facilitate-recovery outcome-inertness test as the enforcing teeth; distinguishes `reconciliation.confirmation-reversed` as a ledger correction, never a financial transaction.
2. **ADR-0035** gained a **"Forward-looking (planned, not built): Donation Contribution Statement"** section: a planned per-member export, not yet scoped to any story, stated as governance intent — the statement **shall be available** to (a) the nominee, for transparency over contributions received, and (b) authorized administrators responsible for reconciliation and contribution confirmation, with trustee read-only access retained as an additional line. Contents: contributor name, contribution amount, contribution date, UTR/reference, cycle, nominee, pool — explicitly excluding bank account numbers, IFSC, phone numbers, internal identifiers, and audit IDs.
3. **ADR-0034** gained a **"Retention scope, stated precisely"** subsection: the 180-day retention window covers ONLY the raw uploaded bank-statement object and its storage-layer metadata; it does NOT cover reconciliation events, contribution history, audit history, or the normalized `bank_statement_entries` rows. On a second pass, the exclusion for those normalized rows was reworded to trustee-legible language: *"these records form part of the trust's reconciliation ledger and therefore follow the trust's financial-record retention policy rather than the raw bank-statement lifecycle this ADR defines"* — replacing an earlier vaguer "ordinary data-retention posture" phrasing.
4. **ADR-0032** and **ADR-0033** each gained a one-line cross-reference distinguishing bank-recorded "reversal" line items (input-data classification, from the source bank statement) from the TWT-side ledger-correction meaning of "reversal" defined in ADR-0035.

**Decision:**
All four ADRs are **RATIFIED**, no further amendments beyond the four pre-presentation revisions above:

1. **ADR-0032 (bank-statement normalization schema) — RATIFIED as authored.** No accepted failure modes beyond normal engineering; the canonical `BankStatementEntry` schema, integer-paise money, and deterministic UUIDv5 `entry_id` stand as shipped.
2. **ADR-0033 (bank-statement intake pipeline) — RATIFIED, with its open risk explicitly carried, not resolved.** The 5-bank CSV-first allowlist ships; the baseline-format assumption remains **STILL UN-ATTESTED** against any real bank export — ratification affirms the pipeline design and its honest fallback (unparseable real files route to staff transcription, never silently mis-parsed), not that the assumption has been verified. Named re-trigger stands: the first real statement upload per bank.
3. **ADR-0034 (object-storage tier policy) — RATIFIED for the bank-statement portion ONLY.** Tier-1 PII classification, the `BankStatementStorage` port, and the 180-day retention window (now precisely scoped, see revision 3 above) are ratified. The KYC-document, death-certificate, and Contribution-Note-PDF portions of this same reserved ADR slot remain **unauthored and unratified** — this ratification does not close them; they stay owned by their own future stories.
4. **ADR-0035 (reconciliation matcher mechanism) — RATIFIED as the highest-stakes item in the batch.** The `matchPool` engine, the monotonic three-layer confirmation invariant, the emission policy, and the AR-45 resilience port are ratified, together with the two new Governance principles (item 1 above) and the Donation Contribution Statement forward-look (item 2 above). The panel noted, without objection, that the initial draft had three real bugs caught and fixed at code review before this presentation (entry-exclusivity, an inert timestamp window, a pre-commit enqueue-ordering bug) — see the consent sheet's Read-first section.

**3-surface cascade applied 2026-08-01 (per ADR):**
- **`ADR-0032-bank-statement-normalization-schema.md`**, **`ADR-0033-bank-statement-intake-pipeline.md`**, **`ADR-0034-object-storage-tier-policy.md`**, **`ADR-0035-reconciliation-matcher-mechanism.md`** — each flipped `Status: drafted → ratified`, `Date` → `2026-08-01 (date entered current status)`, `Ratifying trustees` filled in, `## Ratification (2026-08-01)` section + changelog row appended per file.
- **`adr-index.md`** — all four Section A rows flipped `drafted` → `ratified` (ADR-0034's row keeps its explicit "bank-statement portion ONLY" scope qualifier — ratification does not extend to the KYC/death-cert/Note-PDF portions); status-count breakdown updated (`drafted` 4→0, `ratified` 30→34; Total unchanged at 148) — no `drafted` Section A rows remain.
- **`.decision-log.md`** — this entry.

**Open follow-ups carried forward (NOT closed by this ratification):**
- ADR-0033's baseline-format assumption stays open, un-attested, with its named re-trigger (first real per-bank statement upload) — ratification affirms the design, not the assumption.
- ADR-0034's KYC-document / death-certificate / Contribution-Note-PDF tier-and-lifecycle policy remains unauthored, under its own future story or stories.
- ADR-0035's Donation Contribution Statement is a planned direction only — no story, no code, exists for it today.
- ADR-0035's eight accepted failure modes (from Story 9.4's own review) remain accepted, not resolved, by this ratification.

**References:**
`docs/adr/ADR-0032-bank-statement-normalization-schema.md`, `ADR-0033-bank-statement-intake-pipeline.md`, `ADR-0034-object-storage-tier-policy.md`, `ADR-0035-reconciliation-matcher-mechanism.md` (all ratified) · `docs/knowledge-transfer/adr-index.md` (Section A) · `docs/knowledge-transfer/adr-ratification-consent-sheet-2026-08-01-bank-statement-batch.md` · Stories 9.2/9.3/9.4 · `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/7-6-pool-bound-payment-enforcement.md` + `scripts/pool-bound-payment-invariant/` · `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/9-8-reconciliation-review-queue-ordered-by-alert-deadline-proximity.md` · `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/9-11-over-payment-facilitated-recovery.md` · Decision `2026-08-01-070` (the same-day ADR-0036 ratification preceding this batch)

### Decision 2026-08-01-070: Trustee Panel ratifies ADR-0036 (feature-flag tool selection, with three amendments) and Decision 2026-08-01-069 (capability-bar admission batch, with one condition)

**Decision type:** Governance — ADR ratification (Trustee Panel consent-sheet session) + ratification-with-condition of a prior un-attested-pending decision.
**Status:** Trustee-ratified.
**Author:** Solo Builder (session facilitation)
**Ratifying trustees:** Dhiraj Rahul (Trustee 1) + Kalpana Bharti (Trustee 2) — ≥2-trustee quorum met, 2026-08-01.

**Context:**
ADR-0036 (feature-flag tool selection, Story 10.8) was authored 2026-07-31 and had not yet been presented to the Trustee Panel. Its four capability-bar admission entries all cite it as their ADR reference, and the admission batch itself (Decision `2026-08-01-069`) was recorded the same day as author-committed / un-attested-pending, explicitly coupled to ADR-0036's own ratification. Consent sheet: `docs/knowledge-transfer/adr-ratification-consent-sheet-2026-08-01.md`.

**Decision:**
1. **ADR-0036 is RATIFIED**, with three in-session amendments:
   a. **Decision framing** changed from a vendor-rejection framing ("No third-party feature-flag vendor is adopted for v1") to a scale/governance-fit judgment: *"An in-house implementation is the appropriate solution for the current scale and governance requirements of TWT. The decision should be revisited if future requirements (e.g. experimentation, multi-region operation, large-scale percentage rollouts, or advanced analytics) materially exceed the capabilities of the in-house implementation."* Four named revisit triggers now appear in the ADR's Decision section, alongside (not replacing) the pre-existing engineering-level pivot triggers.
   b. **"Postgres-backed" reframed** as "integrated into TWT's primary transactional platform (Google Cloud SQL for PostgreSQL)" throughout the ADR title, Decision section, and the `adr-index.md` Section A row. The panel's concern: the original framing could read as PostgreSQL being chosen as the defining technology, rather than the subsystem living on the platform TWT already runs, avoiding an additional external feature-flag service.
   c. **"Declared, not production-active" established** as the explicit standing label for the three no-live-consumer capability-bar admissions (see item 2), applied to the ADR's Decision section and mirrored into `governance_boundary.yaml`'s own rationale text.
2. **Decision `2026-08-01-069` (capability-bar admission batch) is RATIFIED WITH ONE CONDITION**: `kyc_provider_selection`, `wa_cost_optimization`, and `telegram_mirror` remain explicitly marked "declared, not production-active" until each ships its first real consumer, and every future review (quarterly flag-inventory audit, this ADR, the admission entries themselves) must continue to distinguish admitted capability from live production behaviour. The panel's own framing, recorded verbatim: *"By ratifying ADR-0036, the Trustee Panel affirms the architectural decision to implement feature flags in-house using the described governance controls. Ratification does not certify that every admitted capability is currently active in production, nor does it waive the accepted limitations listed under 'Read-first priority.'"*
3. **The condition is mechanized, not left as prose alone.** `governance_boundary.yaml`'s `rationale` field for all three affected entries now states "DECLARED, NOT PRODUCTION-ACTIVE (Trustee condition, 2026-08-01)" explicitly; the golden-hash pin in `packages/domain/tests/feature-flags/capability-bar.test.ts` (`EXPECTED_BAR_HASH`) was updated in the same change, so the label cannot silently drift out of the attested artifact without failing CI.

**3-surface cascade applied 2026-08-01:**
- **`ADR-0036-feature-flag-tool-selection.md`** flipped `drafted` → `ratified` (`Status`, `Date` → `2026-08-01 (date entered current status)`, `Ratifying trustees` filled in, `## Ratification (2026-08-01)` section + `## Changelog` table added; title + Decision section amended per items 1a/1b/1c).
- **`adr-index.md`** — ADR-0036 Section A row flipped `drafted` → `ratified`, description reworded per 1a/1b, status-count breakdown updated (`drafted` 5→4, `ratified` 29→30, Total unchanged at 148), ledger note added.
- **`governance_boundary.yaml`** — rationale text for `kyc_provider_selection`, `wa_cost_optimization`, `telegram_mirror` amended per item 3; `count` unchanged at 4 (text-only amendment, no entry added or removed).
- **`packages/domain/tests/feature-flags/capability-bar.test.ts`** — `EXPECTED_BAR_HASH` updated to match the amended rationale text; full `@twt/domain` test suite re-run green (1355 passed, 1 previously-failing golden-hash test now passing on the new pin).

**Open follow-ups carried forward:**
- The condition in item 2 binds ongoing: the "declared, not production-active" label lifts per-flag only when that flag's first real consumer ships (re-triggers: a real AR-43 second KYC provider for `kyc_provider_selection`; the per-Pariwar cost-optimization admin surface for `wa_cost_optimization`; the first live Telegram mirror dispatch for `telegram_mirror`) — each its own story, each its own decision-log entry when it lands.
- ADR-0032/0033/0034/0035 remain `drafted`, explicitly deferred to Story 14.7's AR-69 backlog closure — untouched by this session.
- The eight accepted failure modes in ADR-0036 (AR-64 auto-rollback not built, NFR performance unmeasured, OR-only cohort predicate, dropped scheduled-flip support, the governance gate's non-transitive coverage, etc.) are ratified as *accepted*, not resolved — ratification affirms the control, not a claim that these are closed.

### Decision 2026-08-01-069: Story 10.8 — the feature-flag capability bar admits its first FOUR behaviours; the `governance_boundary.yaml` admission workflow is applied to itself; `kyc_manual_fallback`'s attested fail-safe polarity CORRECTED

**Decision type:** Governance — capability-bar admission (the first application of the `governance_boundary.yaml` admission workflow), plus a correction to an attested rationale.
**Status:** Solo-builder-author-committed; ≥2-trustee ratification pending (recorded un-attested, carried as risk — no backfill)
**Author:** Solo Builder
**Ratifying trustees:** pending

**Context:**
Story 10.8 authored `governance_boundary.yaml` — the closed allowlist of behaviours a feature flag is permitted to toggle — whose own header requires that every admission carry "1. Trustee attestation (recorded in `.decision-log.md`)". The bar shipped with four seeded `allow` entries and **no such record existed**, so the first application of the workflow was missing its own ledger entry. Surfaced by the Story 10.8 Pass-2 Acceptance Auditor. Recording it now rather than treating the omission as immaterial: an admission workflow whose first use skips its own requirement is how the requirement stops being real.

Separately, the same review pass found that one of the four attested rationales asserted the OPPOSITE of what the code did, which makes the attestation itself false and must be corrected in the same entry.

**Decision:**
1. **Admit four behaviours to the capability bar**, each with a named owner, a real or committed consumer, and a `dead_by` date (Story 10.8 Decision 9): `kyc_manual_fallback` (FR-2, `member_flow`, wired end-to-end), `kyc_provider_selection` (AR-43, `provider_selection`, seam wired), `wa_cost_optimization` (AR-18, `channel_routing`, consumer deferred), `telegram_mirror` (FR-73, `channel_routing`, consumer deferred). `count: 4`.
2. **`beta_ux_patterns` is DELIBERATELY NOT ADMITTED** despite appearing in the epic's seed list (`epics.md:3512`). It occurs exactly once in the whole corpus with no FR, AR, or UX-spec backing; an allowlist entry for an undefined behaviour cannot be gate-checked, and admitting it would normalise the silent expansion the bar exists to prevent.
3. **CORRECTION to `kyc_manual_fallback`'s attested rationale.** The shipped rationale read *"The fallback default is `true` (fallback AVAILABLE), so a flag subsystem that says nothing can never silently make KYC mandatory for anyone."* That is false. The flag is named for the CUTOVER, so `fallback_default` answers "is the cutover active?"; with `true`, an unevaluable cohort rule on a persisted row traced through `evaluateFlag` → `enabled: true` → the seam's `!decision.enabled` → the manual-fallback CTA HIDDEN → **KYC hard-mandatory** — precisely the outcome the sentence claimed was impossible. The registry constant is corrected to `false` and the rationale rewritten to state the trace explicitly. The attestation this entry records is of the CORRECTED text.
4. **The ADR reference on all four entries is `ADR-0036`, which is `drafted`, not ratified.** Recorded openly rather than glossed: the entries cite an ADR that has not yet been through trustee ratification, so the attestation chain is complete in FORM but not yet in AUTHORITY.

**Open follow-ups:**
- ≥2-trustee ratification of this admission batch AND of ADR-0036. Until then all four entries are author-committed only.
- Any future addition to the bar requires its own `.decision-log.md` entry, a new `allow` entry, a `count` bump in the same commit, and a matching `FLAG_DEFAULTS` key — the gate asserts registry ≡ allowlist in both directions, so neither half can move alone.
- The capability-bar golden hash now covers each entry's `rationale` prose (previously excluded), so a future silent rewrite of an attestation fails CI rather than passing unnoticed.

**References:**
`governance_boundary.yaml` · `packages/domain/src/feature-flags/registry.ts` (`FLAG_DEFAULTS`) · `scripts/governance-boundary/` · `docs/adr/ADR-0036-feature-flag-tool-selection.md` (drafted) · `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/10-8-feature-flags-per-cohort-capability-bar-governance-boundary-invariant.md` (Review Findings — Pass 2) · Story 10.8 Decision 2 / Decision 9 · `prd.md:884-892` (FR-58C) · `epics.md:3512-3522`

### Decision 2026-07-25-068: Story 8.12 — SM-1 B21 90-second-loop TWT-portion measurement instrument landed; launch-gate roster Row 16 added (`open`, field run un-attested-pending)

**Decision type:** Governance — launch-gate inventory row addition (Story-15-supersession append). Source: epics.md:3055-3076 (Story 8.12) + epics.md:2853 (SM-1 B21 "Measurement fence").
**Status:** Solo-builder-author-committed
**Author:** BigDev (Story 8.12 dev-story)
**Ratifying trustees:** pending (row stays `open`; the `closed` flip is the field-run PASS, not this author-commit)

**Context:**
SM-1 demo beat B21 commits the 90-second contribution loop to a falsifiable **measurement fence**: TWT-portion ≤ 60s p95; UPI-app round-trip reported separately; total observed ≤ 90s. The loop already ships end-to-end (8.2 card → 8.4 UPI Intent + UTR self-attest + yellow pill → 8.13 live nominee-VPA `pa=`). Story 8.12 is `[GOVERNANCE]` — it builds the **measurement instrument** for that loop and registers the pre-launch field run as a launch-gate item, without changing the loop (ZERO member-facing friction / behavior change / PII / hot-path latency; a mark is a `performance.now()` read).

**Decision:**
1. The Story 8.12 measurement instrument is committed: the on-device timing harness (`apps/mobile/lib/loop-timing.ts` pure breakdown + `loop-timing-session.ts` in-flight orchestration + `loop-timing-store.ts` debug-gated MMKV export + the four boundary marks in `_layout.tsx` / `ActiveContributionCard.tsx` / `UPIIntentButton.tsx` / `(contribution)/pay.tsx`) and the off-device p95 aggregation (`apps/jobs/tests/loop-90s-aggregate.ts`, reusing `@twt/measured-validation`'s floor-indexed nearest-rank `percentile()`). All three pure surfaces are unit-tested.
2. Launch-gate roster **Row 16** (`sm1-90s-twt-portion-loop-measurement`) is added below the reserved Row 15 per the append-below-Row-15 / Story-15-supersession convention — the FIRST row ever appended there. Owner = BigDev; `current_status: open`; `architecture_source_line: N/A` (epics/PRD-authored, cited to epics.md per [[feedback_architecture_vs_prd_boundary]]).
3. The ≥ 10-session field run on the canonical validation device under throttled cellular (cold cache) is a manual, on-device launch-gate activity owned by BigDev, before Phase-1 launch. Its results + verdict are scaffolded `_PENDING-MEASUREMENT_` / `_PENDING-FIELD-RUN_` in `_bmad-output/research/contribution-loop-90s-validation.md` and carried **un-attested-pending** — no session figures are fabricated ([[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]]; *Resolved via explicit deferral* per [[feedback_closure_language_precision]]).

**Open follow-ups:**
- BigDev: execute the ≥ 10-session field run; populate the validation doc §5/§6/§7; on PASS flip Row 16 → `closed`; on FAIL (p95 TWT-portion > 60s) remediate before Phase-1 launch (segment (b) offender ⇒ the 8.13 KMS-on-hot-path per the doc §3/D5) then re-measure.
- Trustee disposition at Phase-1 launch if the field run has not executed (`accepted-risk` / `deferred-per-named-criteria`).

### Decision 2026-07-20-067: ADR-0031 ratification — close-of-cycle template-driven framing + Pool-Reality #2 disallowance (FR-19); discharges the Story 7.8 un-attested-pending record

**Decision type:** Governance — ADR ratification (Trustee Panel). Source: ADR-0031 (authored at Story 7.8 closure 2026-07-19).
**Status:** Trustee-ratified
**Author:** BigDev (Solo Builder), presenting
**Ratifying trustees:** Dhiraj Rahul (Trustee 1) + Kalpana Bharti (Trustee 2)

**Context:**
ADR-0031 was author-committed 2026-07-19 at Story 7.8 closure with ratification recorded **un-attested-pending** — no Trustee Panel session was fabricated at closure, per [[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]]. It was the only Section A ADR remaining in the `drafted` state; all 28 others were already `ratified`. This session convened the panel to present it for the ≥2-trustee quorum. Consent sheet: `docs/knowledge-transfer/adr-ratification-consent-sheet-2026-07-20.md`.

The ADR commits the *control mechanism* enforcing the FR-19 *property* (close-of-cycle copy is template-driven; comparison-to-target framing is disallowed) — per [[feedback_architecture_vs_adr_boundary]], the architecture/PRD records the property, the ADR records the enforcement. Presented **policy-adjacent**: the mechanism (a dedicated `close-of-cycle` i18n namespace; a pure exhaustive `selectCloseOfCycleFraming` selector; a target-quarantining `classifyCycleOutcome` classifier; scope+semantic extension of the ADR-0016 microcopy floor and the ADR-0019 tone-review ceiling — no new scanner) is engineering substrate, but the **Pool-Reality #2 disallowance** it enforces is a dignity commitment materially the trust's: on the exact surface a nominee family reads at close-of-cycle, an under-funded close must never surface a comparison-to-target frame.

**Decision:**
1. **ADR-0031 is ratified** (not deferred, not rejected, no amendments) by the ≥2-trustee quorum. The panel affirmed the *structural* control — the target/shortfall figure is quarantined in the classifier and cannot reach the copy-selection path; the `under_funded` branch cannot return a comparison template because the namespace carries no shortfall copy, guarded by the microcopy gate over the two locale files — over a decay-prone per-consumer convention.
2. The un-attested-pending record (author-committed 2026-07-19) is **discharged** — attested by a real quorum, not reconstructed.
3. The 3-surface cascade is applied 2026-07-20: (a) the ADR file flipped `drafted → ratified` (Status/Date/Ratifying-trustees + a `## Ratification (2026-07-20)` section + changelog row); (b) `adr-index.md` Section A row flipped `drafted → ratified`, breakdown updated (`drafted` 1→0, `ratified` 28→29, Total unchanged at 148), ledger note appended; (c) this decision entry.

**Open follow-ups:**
- **`{familyName}` PII policy — deferred to the rendering consumers by design, NOT discharged here.** The template files carry no PII (values arrive as `{token}`s), but `{familyName}` is PII once a surface renders a deceased member's family name publicly. Display scope, consent posture, and publication rules remain Epic 8's (Panchayat Noticeboard) and Epic 11b's (Sahyog Vivran) responsibility, tracked in those stories.
- All Section A ADRs are now `ratified`; no `drafted` rows remain.

**References:**
- `docs/adr/ADR-0031-close-of-cycle-template-driven-framing.md` (ratified) — the control mechanism
- `docs/knowledge-transfer/adr-ratification-consent-sheet-2026-07-20.md` — the consent sheet + Session Resolution
- `docs/knowledge-transfer/adr-index.md` — the authoritative status ledger (Section A row + breakdown)
- ADR-0016 (microcopy automated floor, extended) + ADR-0019 (tone-review human ceiling, wired by the consumer)
- epics.md Story 7.8 + FR-19 (L60) + FR-77 (L146); Decision 2026-07-08-065 (prior ADR ratification batch precedent)

### Decision 2026-07-19-066: FR-20 pool-spawn capacity envelope — pre-launch measured-validation harness landed; Row 13 superseded to `closed`-ready-PENDING-4L (4L number carried un-attested); ≥2-trustee ratification pending

**Decision type:** Governance — substrate/capacity-validation outcome (launch-gate disposition). Source: Story 7.9 (Pool Engine Pre-Launch Measured-Validation Gate).
**Status:** Solo-builder-author-committed; awaiting trustee sign-off — _AWAITING EXTERNAL ACTION_ (operator-run 4L execution + ≥2-trustee ratification)
**Author:** BigDev (solo builder)
**Ratifying trustees:** pending (≥2 at the next monthly review per `closure-criteria-rubric.md` §5)

**Context:**
Launch-gate inventory **Row 13** (`fr-20-pool-spawn-capacity-envelope-conditional`) carried the FR-20 / NFR-7 / AR-68 capacity envelope — the Pool-Spawn saga (Stories 7.1–7.8) must spawn N=50 pools for a Pariwar of M=4L (400,000) active-and-valid members with a p95 wall-clock < 60 s (≥ 10 runs), and the architecture (lines 4830–4840) named a **pre-launch measurement** as the predicate-decision-point: envelope holds → Row 13 → `closed`; envelope fails → Row 13 → `open` + revision ADR. Story 7.9 executes that predicate.

**Decision:**
1. The measured-validation **harness landed** — `apps/jobs/tests/measured-validation-pool-spawn.test.ts` + `apps/jobs/tests/pool-cohort-seed.ts`, reusing the AI-6-2 core (extracted this Story to `@twt/measured-validation`, RATIFIED Path A). It drives the **real** Pool-Spawn saga (parent `planCycleSpawn` → N concurrent full-wire `runCycleSpawnChild` → exactly-once `cycle.frozen`) and records a `schema_version`-stamped p95 record to `_bmad-output/research/pool-engine-validation-gate.md`. Budget pinned at 60_000 ms on every tier (`pass = p95 < budgetMs`; asserted `true`) — no doctored ceiling.
2. **Proven at dev time (attested):** the harness runs end-to-end against real Postgres (`twt-test-pg` :5433) at **smoke** scale, has teeth (the `pass` field flips to `false` on breach — asserted), and the replay co-attestation (assignment-map determinism + `cycle_id` discrimination) passes.
3. **The 4L / N=50 / ≥10-run number is carried UN-ATTESTED** ([[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]]) — full 4L seeding is impractical in the dev environment; the 4L run is operator-executed (`MEASURED_VALIDATION=1` on a representative-scale Postgres) and appends its `pre-launch-4L` record to the evidence doc. It is **not** reconstructed or faked.
4. **Row 13 disposition:** superseded to a **`closed`-ready-PENDING-4L** posture — the `closed`-ready entry + `closure_evidence_link` → the evidence doc are authored, but the `closed` flip remains blocked on (a) the operator 4L record landing with `pass === true`, **and** (b) ≥2-trustee ratification. If the 4L run reveals `pass === false`, Row 13 flips to `open` + a revision ADR — the evidence, not appearance, decides.

**Open follow-ups:**
- Operator-run the 4L / N=50 / ≥10-run measurement (`MEASURED_VALIDATION=1` + representative-scale `DATABASE_URL`); append the `pre-launch-4L` record + Trustee-Panel screenshot/recording to the evidence doc.
- ≥2-trustee ratification of the Row 13 `closed` flip at the next monthly review (or `open` + revision ADR on a breach).

### Decision 2026-07-08-065: ADR ratification batch — ADR-0026 (DigiLocker signature policy), ADR-0027 (push provider), ADR-0028 (WhatsApp provider), ADR-0029 (WA webhook ingress + opt-in lifecycle), ADR-0030 (compensating-audit mechanization)

**Decision type:** Governance — ADR ratification (Trustee Panel consent-sheet session). Source: `docs/knowledge-transfer/adr-ratification-consent-sheet-2026-07-08.md` (reviewed + signed).
**Status:** Trustee-ratified (attested — the consent sheet carries the panel's per-row marks + a signed Session Resolution).
**Author:** BigDev (Solo Builder)
**Ratifying trustees:** Dhiraj Rahul (Trustee 1) + Kalpana Bharti (Trustee 2) — ≥2-trustee quorum met, 2026-07-08.

**Context:**
The 2026-07-08 consent sheet collected trustee ratification for five ADRs that were all `drafted` and un-presented: ADR-0026 (authored 2026-06-25 at Story 3.3a closure), ADR-0027 and ADR-0028 (both authored 2026-07-05, at Stories 5.2 and 5.3 closure respectively), ADR-0029 (authored 2026-07-06 at Story 5.4 closure), and ADR-0030 (authored 2026-07-08 at Epic 5 retrospective AI-5-3 closure, revised through two pre-ratification amendment passes the same day). The panel signed all five rows "Ratify," with no amendments or conditions recorded on any row.

Preparing the sheet surfaced an **index-hygiene gap**, not a ratification-status problem: `docs/knowledge-transfer/adr-index.md` carried a Section A row for ADR-0026 only. ADR-0027, ADR-0028, and ADR-0029 had each been authored and correctly landed `drafted` in `docs/adr/`, but no index row was ever added for them. Per [[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]], this entry does not fabricate an earlier index presence for those three — it records their true authoring dates in the ADR files' own changelogs and adds the index row now, ratified in the same session it is first indexed (mirroring the ADR-0025 precedent for a slot that reaches the index and `ratified` in one event).

**Decision:**

1. **ADR-0026-digilocker-signature-policy — RATIFIED.** Two-window certificate staleness budget: fresh ≤7 days (trusted, no alarm), within-budget 7–30 days (trusted + ops alarm), hard limit >30 days (new KYC verifications fail closed → `pending-valid` manual trustee fallback; existing verified members unaffected); annual trust-anchor/KEK review + daily certificate refresh; key-compromise re-verification queue. Flagged **trustee-judgment** weight on the consent sheet (P0 KYC security policy) — read and ratified as drafted, no amendment.
2. **ADR-0027-push-provider-selection — RATIFIED.** A single `firebase-admin` SDK sends to both Android (FCM HTTP v1) and iOS (APNs) — no native APNs `.p8` credential path, no second auth-refresh mechanism. Light-touch vendor selection behind the Story 5.1 provider-stub abstraction.
3. **ADR-0028-whatsapp-provider-selection — RATIFIED.** The `whatsapp` channel integrates directly with Meta's WhatsApp Business Cloud API for v1 — no BSP intermediary (Gupshup/Twilio/WATI). Light-touch; AR-53 keeps a future BSP swap a single-module change.
4. **ADR-0029-whatsapp-webhook-ingress-and-opt-in-lifecycle — RATIFIED.** Per-Pariwar signed webhook URL (`X-Hub-Signature-256` keyed by the Pariwar's app secret, verification key resolved from the path before the body is trusted); persist-and-ack + async pg-boss worker drain; opt-in lifecycle split across a dedicated state table + the Story 2.7 consent registry. Flagged **policy-adjacent** on the consent sheet for the AR-16 user-initiated-only consent posture — read closely by the panel; no amendment resulted.
5. **ADR-0030-compensating-audit-mechanization — RATIFIED.** Shared `withCompensatingAudit` helper (`packages/domain/src/audit`) closing 5 confirmed compensating-audit gaps in `channel-config`/`degraded-mode`, backfilling 4 existing hand-rolled implementations, and an AST gate enforcing the positive "reaches `withCompensatingAudit`" invariant (gated on rollback-capable transaction-handle presence, not mutation-name heuristics). Light-touch — same family as the already-ratified ADR-0012–0015 CI-gate cluster. Two-commit implementation sequencing recorded in the ADR (§5): Commit 1 — helper + five-site fix; Commit 2 — four-module backfill + AST gate.

**Cascade applied:**
1. `docs/adr/ADR-0026-digilocker-signature-policy.md`, `ADR-0027-push-provider-selection.md`, `ADR-0028-whatsapp-provider-selection.md`, `ADR-0029-whatsapp-webhook-ingress-and-opt-in-lifecycle.md`, `ADR-0030-compensating-audit-mechanization.md` — each `Status: drafted → ratified`, `Date` set to 2026-07-08, `Ratifying trustees` filled in, changelog row appended.
2. `docs/knowledge-transfer/adr-index.md` — ADR-0026 and ADR-0030 rows flipped `drafted` → `ratified`; ADR-0027/0028/0029 added as new Section A rows (the index-hygiene gap) and ratified in the same edit; status-count table corrected and updated (`drafted` 1→0 net, `ratified` 23→28, Total 143→147 — the total also absorbs a same-day bookkeeping correction for the ADR-0030 row added earlier 2026-07-08 without being posted to the count); reconciliation footnote appended per `README.md` §7 ledger-vs-component reconciliation discipline.
3. `.decision-log.md` — this entry.

**Open follow-ups:**
- Implementation of ADR-0030 proceeds per its two-commit sequencing (§5): Commit 1 (helper + five-site fix), then Commit 2 (four-module backfill + AST gate).
- None of the other four ratified ADRs (0026–0029) carry an open follow-up beyond what their own Consequences sections already name (e.g. ADR-0026's quarterly key-compromise-procedure rehearsal, already an existing operational commitment, not new).

### Decision 2026-07-05-064: Trustee consent session — Phase-0 framework ratifications R1-R4 (legal-counsel review scope, KT pack, degradation policy, launch-gate inventory); R5 excluded as already-closed

**Decision type:** Governance — trustee consent-sheet session (Story 0.13 Task 7 + Story 0.5 Task 8 + Story 0.4 Task 7 + Story 0.15 Task 8 ratifications). Source: `docs/knowledge-transfer/trustee-consent-sheet-phase0-framework-ratifications.md` (reviewed + signed).
**Status:** Trustee-ratified (attested — the consent sheet carries the panel's per-row marks + a signed Session Resolution).
**Author:** BigDev (Solo Builder)
**Ratifying trustees:** Dhiraj Rahul (Trustee 1) + Kalpana Bharti (Trustee 2) — ≥2-trustee quorum met, 2026-07-05.

**Context:**
The 2026-07-05 consent sheet collected trustee ratification for the **READY** cluster (R1-R5) of `docs/knowledge-transfer/outstanding-trustee-actions.md`. The panel signed all five rows on the sheet ("Ratified/signed: all"). While running the per-row cascade, R5 (Story 0.14 Task 7 — native-stack experiment scope + device-procurement budget) was found to already be **closed since 2026-06-05** via Decision 2026-06-05-030 (the Q14.1-Q14.4 trustee-questionnaire mechanism) — a fact neither the outstanding-trustee-actions register nor the consent sheet reflected (both carried it forward as still-pending R5). Per [[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]], this entry does **not** record a second ratification of an already-ratified item; it records R1-R4 and corrects the R5 staleness for the trustees' awareness.

**Decision:**

**R1 — Legal-counsel review scope ratified.** `docs/legal-counsel-engagement/review-scope-charter.md` (the 5 AC-named scope items + 32-row cross-Story deferred-scope inventory + 13-row regulatory-surface review + 6-row pre-launch checkpoint coverage) ratified pack-as-a-unit. Discharges Story 0.13 **Task 7**. Recorded in `legal-counsel-engagement/engagement-ledger.md` §3. **Unblocks B1** — counsel shortlist + selection (Task 8) may now begin; this is the critical-path item per the register.

**R2 — Knowledge-Transfer pack ≥2-trustee sign-off.** `docs/knowledge-transfer/` components (ADR-index, Niyamavali→FR mapping, deployment-topology, on-call-playbook, third-party-dependency-inventory) + comprehension questionnaire + answer key, signed pack-as-a-unit. Discharges Story 0.5 **Task 8**. Recorded in `kt-pack-ledger.md` §2 (two trustee rows, quorum met); framework-commit row status superseded `Author-committed; awaiting trustee sign-off` → `Trustee-ratified`. Task 9 (comprehension-questionnaire administration to the backup engineer) stays open, gated on Story 0.6 (B3).

**R3 — Degradation policy ≥2-trustee sign-off (framework; comms-templates carve-out).** `docs/degradation-policy/` surface-inventory + table-top-exercise runbook, signed per-bundle. Discharges Story 0.4 **Task 7 for the framework only**. Recorded in `degradation-policy-ledger.md` Trustee sign-off log (two rows). Per closure-language precision, this is **not** a full AC-1 closure: the 5 comms templates carry `PENDING LEGAL REVIEW` and re-sign individually once Story 0.13 counsel returns (B1) — recorded as still-open, not asserted closed.

**R4 — Architectural launch-gate inventory ratified + monthly-review cadence armed.** `docs/launch-gate-inventory/inventory-roster.md` (15-row roster) + `monthly-review-cadence-protocol.md` ratified as the tracker + review process. Discharges Story 0.15 **Task 8** and arms **Task 9** (first monthly review, now due). Recorded in `launch-gate-inventory/engagement-ledger.md` §3. This ratifies the *tracker*, not the individual rows — per-row status is unchanged. **Roster Rows 1+2** (already `in-progress` pending this exact ratification per Decision 2026-06-03-015 D-01) may now be evaluated for closure at the first monthly review.

**Slip recorded (D-03 discipline, `engagement-ledger.md` §3):** Task 8 ratification was due on or before **2026-07-01** (Decision 2026-06-03-015 D-03, 4 weeks from Story 0.15 Task 7 author-commit). It occurred **2026-07-05** — a **4-day slip**. Slip reason: session-scheduling delay; no roster row was affected by the delay and no ratified content changed as a result. Per `monthly-review-cadence-protocol.md` §3, this is surfaced as the first agenda item of the now-convened first monthly review rather than silently absorbed.

**R5 — EXCLUDED; correction recorded, not a new ratification.** Story 0.14 Task 7 (native-stack experiment scope + device-procurement budget) was ratified **2026-06-05** via Decision 2026-06-05-030 (Q14.1-Q14.4), a full month before this session. At that same session Task 8 (device procurement) and Task 9 Days 1-9 (prototype build) also closed — materially further along than either `outstanding-trustee-actions.md` (as-of 2026-06-24) or today's consent sheet reflected. **No new ratification is recorded for R5 here.** The genuinely open Story 0.14 items are Task 10 (Solo Builder measurement collection — not yet run) and Task 11 (ratify-or-pivot decision + ≥1-trustee acknowledgement, per UX spec line 845) — tracked as B5, unchanged by this session.

**Cascade applied:**
1. `docs/legal-counsel-engagement/engagement-ledger.md` §3 — R1 ratification row.
2. `docs/knowledge-transfer/kt-pack-ledger.md` §2 + §1 — R2 sign-off rows + framework-commit status supersession.
3. `docs/degradation-policy/degradation-policy-ledger.md` Trustee sign-off log — R3 sign-off rows (framework; templates carve-out noted).
4. `docs/launch-gate-inventory/engagement-ledger.md` §3 — R4 ratification row + slip note.
5. Story files `0-13`, `0-5`, `0-4`, `0-15` — Task 7/7/7/8 checkboxes flipped to closed, citing this entry.
6. `docs/knowledge-transfer/outstanding-trustee-actions.md` — R1-R4 moved to "recently cleared"; R5 entry corrected to reflect its true 2026-06-05 closure; B1 marked newly unblocked; as-of date refreshed.
7. `.decision-log.md` — this entry.

**Open follow-ups (gated — do NOT assert as closed):**
- **[GATE] B1 — legal-counsel selection (Story 0.13 Task 8)** now unblocked; engagement letter + NDA + COI (Task 9) follow selection.
- **[GATE] Story 0.5 Task 9** — comprehension-questionnaire administration, gated on Story 0.6 backup-engineer onboarding (B3).
- **[GATE] Story 0.4 comms-templates re-sign** — gated on Story 0.13 counsel return (B1).
- **[GATE] Story 0.15 Task 9** — first monthly launch-gate review, now due; agenda includes the 4-day slip acknowledgement.
- **[GATE] Story 0.14 Task 10 + Task 11** — measurement collection + ratify-or-pivot decision, unaffected by this session (B5).

### Decision 2026-06-24-063: Retire the AI-cadence instrument (instrumentation-only) — re-attestation obligation released; 1.497 finding stands

**Decision type:** Other — governance instrument retirement
**Status:** Trustee-ratified
**Author:** BigDev (Solo Builder)
**Ratifying trustees:** Dhiraj Rahul + Kalpana Bharti (same panel as Decisions 2026-06-24-061/062)

**Context:**
The AI-cadence instrument (`ai-cadence-actuals.md` + the `cadence-check` CI gate), stood up per Epic 1 retrospective AI-1 to instrument the 80 hr/wk NET assumption behind `ceiling_ratio = 1.497` (which cleared the `max(floor_ratio, ceiling_ratio) > 1.5` trigger by 0.003), was found at the Epic 2 retrospective to be perpetually un-populated: the gate asserts file existence + currency, NOT row population. Root cause — the instrument proved non-capturable in a solo + AI workflow (session wall-time ≠ net output; idle/context-switch time not cleanly separable), so the re-attestation obligation cannot be satisfied with trustworthy data. Drives Epic 2 retrospective AI-2-1.

**Decision:**
1. Retire the AI-cadence instrument — instrumentation only: remove the `cadence-check` gate (`scripts/cadence-check/check.ts`, the `cadence:check` script, the `ci.yml` job, the `ci-local.sh` registration) and archive `ai-cadence-actuals.md` as RETIRED in place.
2. Release the per-epic re-attestation obligation (it cannot be satisfied with trustworthy data in this workflow).
3. No backfill — Epic 0/1/2 remain recorded UN-ATTESTED (record-un-attested-no-backfill discipline).
4. The Epic-0 `ceiling_ratio = 1.497` no-trigger finding (Decision 2026-06-04-016) STANDS as ratified; `reconciliation-decision-framework.md §1` and its 1.5× trigger are unchanged. Instrumentation-only retirement.
Recorded in ADR-0025-retire-ai-cadence-instrument (`ratified`).

**Open follow-ups:**
- None on this decision. Related Epic 2 retrospective items AI-2-2 (domain-accessor-invariant gate) and AI-2-3 (test-quality checkpoint) are tracked in `epic-2-retro-2026-06-24.md`.

**References:**
- `docs/adr/ADR-0025-retire-ai-cadence-instrument.md`
- `.decision-log.md` Decision 2026-06-04-016 (80 hr/wk cadence sign-off)
- `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/epic-1-retro-2026-06-20.md` (AI-1) · `epic-2-retro-2026-06-24.md` (AI-2-1, H-1) · `ai-cadence-actuals.md` (archived RETIRED)
- `docs/spec-to-cadence-reconciliation/reconciliation-decision-framework.md §1` (unchanged)

### Decision 2026-06-24-062: ADR ratification — ADR-0022 ratified at the 2026-06-24 Trustee Panel session + three in-session governance amendments

**Decision type:** ADR ratification (architectural) + governance amendments — ratifies ADR-0022 (public Astro SSR shell + cache-safe public render) with three trustee-directed amendments incorporated into the ADR.
**Status:** Trustee-ratified (attested — trustee marks and amendment directives received 2026-06-24).
**Author:** BigDev (Solo Builder)
**Ratifying trustees:** Dhiraj Rahul (Trustee 1) + Kalpana Bharti (Trustee 2) — ≥2-trustee quorum met.

**Context:**
ADR-0022 was authored at Story 2.5 closure (2026-06-21) and remained `drafted` — it was noted in Decision 2026-06-21-059 as "authored but not presented." The Trustee Panel ratified it at the 2026-06-24 session (same session as Decision 061) and directed three amendments before the cascade was applied. All three amendments are incorporated directly into the ADR file; no open follow-ups were created.

**Decision:**

ADR-0022 (`public-astro-ssr-shell-cache-safety`) **ratified**, prior status `drafted`, weight trustee-judgment (first public unauthenticated surface; the architecture's mandatory "committed in an ADR" cache-safety artifact).

**Governance amendments adopted in-session:**

- **A. Accepted Risk section.** An explicit `## Accepted Risk` section is added to the ADR formalising the direct-DB-read risk: four structural mitigations enumerated (role+RLS, public-tier-only, write-credential prohibition, PII scrape gate); Epic 11b revisit trigger recorded. The risk was previously mentioned in Consequences/Negative; it is now a named, structured section.
- **B. Write-capable credential prohibition.** §3 (Unauthenticated read-scope) now explicitly states: write-capable credentials must not be issued to or configured in `apps/public`; the database role must carry `SELECT`-only grants. This is a provisioning constraint, not merely code convention.
- **C. Cache key completeness rule.** §2 (Cache-safe public SSR guarantee) now includes an explicit rule: cache keys must include all language selection inputs (`Accept-Language` header + `?lang=` query parameter override). Failure to do so constitutes cache poisoning via locale variation; `Vary: Accept-Language` + distinct `?lang=` URLs are the enforcement mechanism.

**Cascade applied (3 surfaces):**
1. **ADR-0022 file** — `Status → ratified`, `Ratifying trustees` populated, three amendments applied in-ADR, `## Ratification (2026-06-24)` section appended summarising the amendments.
2. **`adr-index.md`** — ADR-0022 Section A row flipped `drafted → ratified` with this decision cross-linked and the 3 amendments noted in the cell; breakdown updated (`drafted` 1→0, `ratified` 21→22); line-17 ledger note updated. **All 22 Section-A ADRs are now `ratified`; no `drafted` rows remain.**
3. **`.decision-log.md`** — this entry.

**Open follow-ups:**
- **Epic 11b** — evaluate whether to adopt the thin-public-API data-path alternative (keeps DB creds out of the public renderer). Not a trustee gate; BigDev judgment call.

**References:**
- `docs/adr/ADR-0022-public-astro-ssr-shell-cache-safety.md`
- `docs/knowledge-transfer/adr-index.md` (Section A row + breakdown)
- Decision 2026-06-21-059 (prior batch — ADR-0022 noted as "authored but not presented") · Decision 2026-06-24-061 (same session — ADR-0023/0024 ratification)
- Memory: [[feedback_architecture_vs_adr_boundary]] · [[feedback_closure_language_precision]]

### Decision 2026-06-23-060: Trustee consent session — 9 runbook authority sign-offs + empty/skeleton/error inventory ratification + escrow sealing authorization + a deploy/rollback execution-validation directive

**Decision type:** Governance — trustee consent-sheet session (runbook authority + artifact ratification + escrow authorization + execution-validation directive). Source: `docs/knowledge-transfer/trustee-consent-sheet-2026-06-23.md` (reviewed + signed).
**Status:** Trustee-ratified (attested — the consent sheet carries the panel's per-row marks + a signed Session Resolution).
**Author:** BigDev (Solo Builder)

**Context:**
The 2026-06-23 consent sheet collected trustee consent for the signature-blocked governance backlog. The panel signed every row. This entry records the session and runs the 3-surface cascade per the sheet's "what I do per signed row".

**Decision:**

**Group A — runbook authority: ≥2-trustee sign-off (9 runbooks).** Signed against git SHA `f247e6d`; recorded in `docs/runbooks/operational-readiness-ledger.md` sign-off table; each runbook header flipped `draft → signed-off`:
`deploy.md`, `rollback.md`, `multi-pariwar-provisioning.md` (the three AS-BUILT Story-1.15 reconciliations), `secret-rotation.md`, `audit-log-integrity-verification.md`, `reconciliation-manual-intervention.md`, `rbac-seed-reset.md`, `job-queue-operations.md`, `trustee-credential-loss-succession.md`.
- Signing `deploy.md` + `rollback.md` + `multi-pariwar-provisioning.md` **discharges the ADR-0011 co-requisite** (Decision 2026-06-21-050) — the 3 reconciled runbooks are now ≥2-trustee re-signed.
- Signing `trustee-credential-loss-succession.md` **discharges the Decision 2026-06-21-059 amendment-A** "succession runbook before production go-live" gate (the document gate; the execution-validation directive below is a separate, still-open gate).

A gap found in execution re-opens the corresponding runbook's sign-off via the AC-3 fix → re-sign → re-execute protocol.

**Discharges:** ADR-0011 co-requisite (Decision 2026-06-21-050) · Decision 2026-06-21-059 amendment-A document gate · the un-attested-pending ratification leg of Decision 2026-06-21-058.

**Governance amendments adopted in-session (the canonical record; the ADR files carry pointers here):**

- **A. Super Admin governance model (amends ADR-0009 admin-authentication).** (1) Super Admin manages ordinary admin accounts. (2) Super Admin assignment requires trustee approval. (3) At least one active trustee must continuously hold Super Admin access. (4) No single non-trustee individual may be the sole holder of Super Admin privileges. **Follow-up (gated):** a trustee credential-loss & succession runbook is **required before production go-live**, covering lost passkeys, exhausted recovery codes, trustee death / resignation / incapacity, and Super Admin transfer/revocation procedures.
- **B. OQ-3 resolved (amends ADR-0008 rbac-permission-model).** (1) The 12-role catalogue is approved. (2) The seed permission matrix is approved **subject to implementation of the trustee-approved amendments**. (3) Future permission additions for Finance Officer, Media/Comms, Field Worker, and Helpline Operator are to be ratified when their feature sets ship. The ADR-0008 "provisional pending OQ-3" qualifier is now resolved-with-conditions (no longer open).
- **C. Niyamavali workflow governance clarification (ADR-0021).** The audit-logged draft→publish workflow is the **authoritative path** by which Niyamavali amendments become official and publishable within the platform.

**Cascade applied (3 surfaces, per the consent-sheet "what I do per ratified row"):**
1. **ADR files** — each of the 18 files: `Status → ratified`, `Date 2026-06-21`, `Ratifying trustees` populated, changelog row appended where a changelog table exists; ADR-0008 OQ-3 consequence annotated resolved; ADR-0009 gains a Ratification section carrying amendment A.
2. **`adr-index.md`** — 18 rows flipped to `ratified` with this decision cross-linked; status-count breakdown reconciled to internally-consistent values (`ratified` 1→19, `drafted` →1 [ADR-0022 only], `under-trustee-review` →0). The reconciliation also corrects a pre-existing breakdown drift (the 2026-06-12 ADR-0009 `under-trustee-review` flip and the 2026-06-21 ADR-0022 add were never posted to the breakdown — its parts summed to 139 vs a stated Total of 140); documented in the line-17 ledger note. Per [[feedback_closure_language_precision]] the drift correction is recorded, not silently absorbed.
3. **`.decision-log.md`** — this entry.

**Open follow-ups (gated — do NOT assert as closed):**
- **[GATE] Trustee credential-loss & succession runbook** before production go-live (amendment A follow-up). Un-gated action items decay, so this is gated to production go-live.
- **OQ-3 amendment implementation** — the seed permission-matrix amendments approved in amendment B must be implemented before the production RBAC seed; future-role permission additions ratified per feature-set ship.
- **[GATE] ADR-0011 co-requisite** — the 3 reconciled runbooks still await ≥2-trustee re-sign (Decision 2026-06-21-050); tracked there, NOT discharged here.

### Decision 2026-06-21-058: P0-4 Empty/Skeleton/Error Inventory — Story 2.5 `apps/public` surfaces authored + author-committed; ≥2-trustee ratification recorded un-attested-pending

**Decision type:** Launch-gate artifact (UX deliverable) — discharges the re-homed P0-4 architectural gate (`inventory-roster.md` Row 6) for the Story 2.5 surface set. Re-home origin: Decision 2026-06-20-054 (Epic-1-retro AI-3).
**Status:** Solo-builder-author-committed. **≥2-trustee ratification: un-attested-pending** (see Open follow-ups).
**Author:** BigDev (Solo Builder)

**Context:**
Architectural P0-4 = "Empty/Skeleton/Error Inventory" (architecture L4783) was committed to Story 1.17 (Epic-0 A-4) but Story 1.17 shipped without it. Decision 2026-06-20-054 (AI-3) RE-HOMED it to **Story 2.5** — the first public surface (`apps/public` Astro SSR shell) — with the binding that the inventory cover every `apps/public` screen surface built at 2.5. Story 2.5 has now landed those surfaces (the public Niyamavali list + version/diff sub-views + the shell's 404/500 routes).

2. **Skeleton/loading recorded N/A by design.** Every `apps/public` route is fully server-rendered with no client fetch and no hydration (`js_bundle_bytes: 0`), so there is no client loading phase to skeleton. This is recorded with rationale, not left blank — a faithful "N/A", not a gap.
3. **Inventory is PARTIAL at 2.5.** It covers `apps/public` only; it is extended at Epic 11a (Member Directory) and Epic 11b (per-claim + In Memoriam). `inventory-roster.md` Row 6 flips `open` → **`in-progress`** (NOT `closed`) — closure is reserved for the full Phase-1 surface inventory at Epic 11a completion (Row 6 closure_criteria preserved verbatim).
4. **≥2-trustee ratification recorded UN-ATTESTED-PENDING.** Per [[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]], the dev workflow authors + author-commits the artifact; it does NOT reconstruct a Trustee-Panel ratification event that did not occur in-session. The ≥2-trustee ratification leg is carried openly as a **gated open follow-up** below — integrity over appearance. This is honest about the closure state: artifact = Closed by [edit]; trustee ratification = Not yet attested.

**Open follow-ups:**
- **[GATE] ≥2-trustee ratification of this inventory** (the Row 6 closure_criteria signature). Convene the Trustee Panel (Dhiraj Rahul + Kalpana Bharti per the Decision 2026-06-21-057 panel) to ratify `docs/ux/empty-skeleton-error-inventory.md`; on ratification, append a successor decision recording the attested ratification and (at Epic 11a full-surface completion) flip Row 6 `in-progress` → `closed`.
