### Decision 2026-05-29-004: Per-surface degradation policy + five-channel comms templates (PENDING LEGAL REVIEW per Story 0.13) + table-top exercise runbook scaffolded; trustee sign-off + first table-top execution pending

**Decision type:** Story 0.4 — Degradation Policy
**Status:** Author-committed; awaiting trustee sign-off
**Author:** Solo Builder (BigDev)
**Ratifying trustees:** _pending_

**Context.** Story 0.4 of Epic 0 commits the per-surface degradation policy per AR-67 + PRD §9.1.1 paragraph 4 ("Degradation policy — if the Solo Builder is unavailable for > 7 days, what does the trust do? Documented per surface … Communications template prepared") + architecture Cross-Cutting #20 ("Solo-build operational continuity — runbooks, credential escrow, code escrow … degradation policy") + Cross-Cutting #9 ("Staff-fallback at every node") + §3.4 (three-tier channel hierarchy) + §5.7 (DR runbook posture) + §5.10 (Solo Builder on-call + backup engineer A-13) + §5.15 (operational runbook inventory) + UX Stance #1 + #4 + #5 + #6 + UX §Phase-0 Prerequisite P0-1 + UX §Surface Priority.

This entry records the author-commit step (Story 0.4 Tasks 1-6, author-committable: scaffold `docs/degradation-policy/`; author the surface inventory; author the 5 channel comms templates with PENDING LEGAL REVIEW markers; author the table-top exercise runbook; cross-link to Story 0.1's operational-readiness-ledger + Story 0.2's credential-inventory `dr-runbook-pdf-custody` row + Story 0.3's code-escrow README + Story 0.1's runbook README "Related runbooks" table + Story 0.2's escrow README "Related escrow surfaces" table). The Trustee Panel sign-off on per-surface stance (Task 7), first table-top exercise execution + gap-list remediation cycle (Task 8), and Story 0.13 comms-template legal-counsel ratification (Task 9) are `_AWAITING EXTERNAL ACTION_` and will be recorded as separate `.decision-log.md` entries that supersede the "pending" status on the relevant fields of this entry per the Story 0.1 + 0.2 + 0.3 schema (supersession is recorded as a new entry referencing the prior; the prior is not modified).

**Decision.**

1. `docs/degradation-policy/` is the canonical location for the degradation-policy framework — a NEW top-level surface under `docs/`, parallel to `docs/escrow/`, `docs/runbooks/`, and `docs/adr/` (the latter pending scaffold per Decision 2026-05-29-003 Open Follow-up #6). Rationale: the framework is broader than a single runbook (multi-file inventory + 5 channel templates + ledger + runbook); the architecture §5.15 runbook inventory does not enumerate the degradation policy as a runbook; the Story 0.2 + 0.3 precedent of framework-as-top-level-surface is the dominant pattern. The choice is recorded in `docs/degradation-policy/README.md` §1 inline so a future reviewer finds the rationale without consulting this entry.

2. The five framework documents — `README.md`, `surface-inventory.md`, `degradation-policy-ledger.md`, `table-top-exercise.md`, and the `comms-templates/` sub-directory (containing `push-channel.md`, `whatsapp-channel.md`, `sms-channel.md`, `email-channel.md`, `public-page-banner.md`) — are the governance instruments for degradation-policy authority. The table-top-exercise runbook uses the five-section template Story 0.1 established (`docs/runbooks/_template.md`); the ledger mirrors the Story 0.1 + 0.2 + 0.3 ledger patterns; the inventory follows the Story 0.2 + 0.3 inventory pattern.

3. The **per-surface stance inventory** enumerates surfaces across UX Tier 1 (member-class) + Tier 2 (operator) + Tier 3 (public) + Account State Machine render-mode sub-rows. v1-in-scope surfaces are populated with `drafted` status and the placeholder `P0-1-pending` in the fallback_handler column (Story 0.7 closure triggers the backfill). Surfaces owned by Stories not yet closed (Module Marketplace per Epic 12; disaster handling per Epic 14; banking integration; advanced reconciliation; DR runbook itself per architecture §5.7) are explicitly out-of-scope at author-commit and will be amended in under the supersession schema as the owning Stories close.

4. **PENDING LEGAL REVIEW marker on every comms template** is committed as a load-bearing structural invariant — silent unflipping is a framework violation. The marker is removed ONLY via `degradation-policy-ledger.md` "Legal-counsel revision log" + trustee co-sign + supersession-schema marker per the README §4 invariant 6. Flipping is gated by Story 0.13 closure + per-template counsel ratification logged in the ledger.

5. **Structural invariants enumerated** in `docs/degradation-policy/README.md` §4: (i) no punitive auto-action per UX Stance #5; (ii) no loop node ships without funded fallback handler per UX Stance #6 (P0-1-pending placeholder until Story 0.7 closes); (iii) no bulk-alert SMS per PRD addendum RA-29 (SMS template fires only via per-member transactional fallback OR Pariwar-degraded-mode cycle-open bridge per architecture §3.4); (iv) no module-promotion under frozen states per UX Stance #1 (Module Shelf grief-context exclusion); (v) no PII / member-state in public-page banner per architecture §5.8a cache-safe SSR guarantee; (vi) PENDING LEGAL REVIEW marker preservation. The Channel-hierarchy invariant cross-check (README §5) and the Account State Machine cross-check (README §6) commit additional load-bearing rules — degraded posture does NOT introduce a fifth SMS fire-condition; degraded posture does NOT introduce a `degraded-frozen` state class.

6. **30-day takeover joint-discharge** anchored in `docs/degradation-policy/degradation-policy-ledger.md` "Table-top exercise log" section header — the Decision 2026-05-29-003 Open Follow-up regarding the AR-67 + PRD §9.1.1 30-day-takeover joint-discharge is **partially evidenced** by Story 0.4 AC-1 (per-surface stance + comms templates + trustee sign-off — the trust knows what the surfaces do under reduced bandwidth) + AC-2 (table-top exercise — the panel can execute the degraded posture without ad-hoc improvisation). The end-to-end 30-day takeover is jointly discharged by Story 0.3 AC-1 + Story 0.3 AC-2 + Story 0.4 AC-1 + Story 0.4 AC-2 + Story 0.5 (KT pack) + Story 0.6 (backup engineer contract). A closure of any one of those six MUST NOT be conflated with the joint discharge per [[feedback_closure_language_precision]].

7. **Legal counsel dependency** — Story 0.13 closure is the unblock for comms-template ratification. The dependency is structural (the PENDING LEGAL REVIEW marker preservation rule), not merely procedural. Story 0.4 may reach `trustee-signed-off` status (Task 7) and `table-top-exercised` status (Task 8) BEFORE Story 0.13 returns; the comms-template `fully-ratified` status is per-template and gated by per-template counsel return. Per-template provisional closure is acceptable in the partial-return window.

8. The framework is **ready** for Tasks 7-9 (`_AWAITING EXTERNAL ACTION_`); Tasks 7-9 close per the AC text in the Story 0.4 file with provisional-vs-full closure language precision per [[feedback_closure_language_precision]]. AC-1 full closure requires Task 7 (≥2-trustee sign-off; per-bundle OR per-surface for ≥80% of v1-in-scope surfaces per the README §7 + ledger 80/20 closure rule) + Task 9 closure for the comms-template legal-counsel ratification leg; provisional closure permitted in the partial-return window. AC-2 full closure requires Task 8 (table-top exercise) + at least one re-execution surfacing "no new gaps" + Story 0.6 closure for the non-Solo-Builder facilitator preference; provisional closure when ≥1 exercise complete with gap list recorded.

**Open follow-ups.**

- Trustee Panel sign-off on per-surface stance + comms templates (Story 0.4 Task 7) per the README §7 ratification-mode choice (per-bundle OR per-surface).
- First table-top exercise execution + gap-list remediation cycle (Story 0.4 Task 8) per the README §9 review cadence + the table-top-exercise runbook §2 7-day scenario script.
- Legal-counsel return on `push-channel.md` (Story 0.4 Task 9; gated by Story 0.13 closure).
- Legal-counsel return on `whatsapp-channel.md` (Story 0.4 Task 9; gated by Story 0.13 closure + Meta UTILITY template approval at template-submission time per architecture §3.4 template approval lead-time policy).
- Legal-counsel return on `sms-channel.md` (Story 0.4 Task 9; gated by Story 0.13 closure + per-template DLT-transactional registration per architecture §2.2 + §3.4).
- Legal-counsel return on `email-channel.md` (Story 0.4 Task 9; gated by Story 0.13 closure + email-provider selection ADR).
- Legal-counsel return on `public-page-banner.md` (Story 0.4 Task 9; gated by Story 0.13 closure + cache-safe SSR review by counsel per architecture §5.8a).
- Operations-policy authoring — quarterly re-attestation cadence + paging integration cadence + post-incident review cadence + on-rotation-event cred re-seal cadence (forward-deferred; no current owning Story).
- Story 0.7 closure → P0-1 fallback-handler citation backfill across `surface-inventory.md` rows (replace `P0-1-pending` placeholder with the P0-1 ledger row cite per the README §4 invariant 2).
- Story 0.6 closure → non-Solo-Builder facilitator path for table-top execution (replace substitute-engineer path with backup-engineer facilitator per the table-top-exercise runbook §1 facilitator-selection).
- **30-day takeover joint-discharge** condition per Decision 2026-05-29-003 Open Follow-up: Story 0.3 AC-1 + Story 0.3 AC-2 + Story 0.4 AC-1 + Story 0.4 AC-2 + Story 0.5 + Story 0.6 jointly discharge the AR-67 + PRD §9.1.1 30-day takeover property. The joint-discharge contribution is recorded per row in `degradation-policy-ledger.md` Table-top exercise log; when all six conditions close, a follow-up `.decision-log.md` entry records the joint-discharge achievement.
- **DR runbook authoring scope decision** — architecture §5.7 commits a DR runbook posture; the `dr-runbook-pdf-custody` row in `docs/escrow/credential-inventory.md` cites Story 0.4 as a candidate owner. Whether Story 0.4 expands its scope OR a dedicated DR-runbook Story authors the DR runbook is a future `.decision-log.md` decision pending. Recommended path: defer to a dedicated DR-runbook Story because the degradation policy is structurally distinct from infrastructure DR per architecture §5.7.
- **Incident-response post-mortem hook** — live degraded-posture activation triggers a within-30-days policy-revision review per the README §9 review cadence fallback. Operations-policy authoring is the long-term owner; until then the fallback cadence stands.
- **Hindi-native trustee ratification path** (Open ADR slot in `docs/degradation-policy/README.md` §8) — if the Trustee Panel lacks a Hindi-native trustee at sign-off time, the Hindi copy ratification is partial; a Story-0.13-engaged counsel review of the Hindi copy is doubly load-bearing in that case.
- **Public-page banner dismissal at authenticated surfaces** (Open ADR slot in §8) — Story 2.5 / 11a.5 territory; the boundary is documented so a future Story does not violate the cache-safe SSR property per architecture §5.8a.
- **Comms-template renderer escaping CI test** (Open ADR slot in §8) — Story 5.x deliverable per Epic 5 alert dispatcher framework; the test asserts that template variable substitution renders as inert text in each channel per architecture §3.4 "Channel-renderer escaping discipline".
- **Sole-Solo-Builder admin attestation on degradation-policy artifacts** (Open ADR slot in §8) — analogous to the Story 0.3 trustee-administrative-control attestation gap on the mirror destination; deferred to Story 1.16a friction-budget PR CI gate.

### Decision 2026-05-29-003: Code-escrow auto-mirror pipeline scaffolded; mirror destination + restoration drill + bus-factor table-top pending

**Decision type:** Story 0.3 — Code Escrow
**Status:** Author-committed; awaiting trustee sign-off
**Author:** Solo Builder (BigDev)
**Ratifying trustees:** _pending_

**Context:**
Story 0.3 of Epic 0 commits the code-escrow framework + the first GitHub Actions workflow in the repo per AR-67 + PRD §9.1.1 + architecture §5.4 ("Source-code host: GitHub primary; escrow mirror to trustee-controlled location per Step 2 §9.1.1"). This entry records the author-commit step (Story 0.3 Tasks 1–6, author-committable: scaffold `docs/escrow/code-escrow/`, author the mirror workflow, define the release-branch set as a property commitment with Story 1.1 amendment trigger, document §2.10a-adjacent surface separation, update Story 0.1's Mirror coverage section + Story 0.2's Related escrow surfaces row + Cloudflare row Notes). The Trustee Panel + Solo Builder selection of mirror destination + credential model (Task 7), GitHub Actions secret wiring (Task 8), ≥2-trustee read-access verification (Task 9), restoration drill (Task 10), and bus-factor switch-to-mirror exercise (Task 11) are `_AWAITING EXTERNAL ACTION_` and will be recorded as separate `.decision-log.md` entries that supersede the "pending" status on the relevant fields of this entry per the Story 0.1 schema (supersession recorded as a new entry referencing the prior; the prior is not modified).

**Decision:**
1. `docs/escrow/code-escrow/` is the canonical location for code-escrow governance instruments — a sub-directory of `docs/escrow/` (which architecture line 4172 commits as "Credential + code escrow inventory + procedures"). The Story 0.2 framework owns the parent directory's existing files; Story 0.3 creates the sub-directory to structurally separate the two surfaces (different ledgers, different inventories, different procedures).
2. The five framework documents — `README.md`, `mirror-destination-inventory.md`, `code-escrow-ledger.md`, `mirror-procedure.md`, `restoration-procedure.md` — are the governance instruments for code-escrow authority. The two runbooks use the five-section template Story 0.1 established (`docs/runbooks/_template.md`); the ledger mirrors the Story 0.1 + 0.2 ledger patterns; the inventory follows the Story 0.2 inventory pattern.
3. `.github/workflows/code-escrow-mirror.yml` is the **first GitHub Actions workflow file in the repo**, authored under Story 0.3 authority. The workflow targets release-branch pushes (release-branch set = `{main}` placeholder; see Decision 4), executes `git push --mirror` to the destination via the wired `MIRROR_PUSH_CREDENTIAL` + `MIRROR_DESTINATION_URL` secrets, runs under `timeout-minutes: 8` to fast-fail before the 10-minute SLA breach, and emits a structured ledger artifact for reconciliation. Story 1.1 (turborepo-monorepo-bootstrap) is expected to ADD the other workflow files (`ci.yml`, `deploy-dev.yml`, etc.) alongside this one — it will NOT replace this workflow.
4. **Release-branch set = `{main}` is a placeholder.** The property committed is "every release-branch push triggers a mirror within 10 minutes." The control (which branches are release branches) is committed by Story 1.1 + architecture §5.4. When Story 1.1 commits the canonical branching model, the workflow's `on: push: branches:` list is amended under a future `.decision-log.md` `[CONTINUITY]` entry citing Story 1.1 closure as the trigger.
5. **Mirror destination is not yet selected.** It is ADR territory and a future Trustee Panel + Solo Builder decision. Candidate destinations (informational; the Trustee Panel decision is the authority) — GitLab.com under trustee-owned account; Codeberg; self-hosted Gitea on trustee-controlled VPS; Bitbucket under foundation account; hybrid dual-mirror — are listed in `docs/escrow/code-escrow/README.md` §"Open ADR slots" and Story 0.3 Task 7. The mirror workflow tags the destination-configuration step `[deferred ADR — placeholder procedure]` until the ADR lands.
6. **Mirror credential model is not yet selected.** Same status as Decision 5. Candidates: deploy-key SSH; OAuth token with `repo:write` scope; platform-native push-mirror feature; hybrid. Selection interacts with the architecture §5.9 rotation cadence.
7. **§2.10a-adjacent surface separation is a load-bearing structural invariant for the code-mirror.** Per `docs/escrow/code-escrow/README.md` §"Surface separation": the mirror carries **source code only** — no GCP service-account keys, no container images, no Artifact Registry artifacts, no audit-mirror data, no production data. Compromise of the mirror destination MUST NOT permit read of audit-mirror data, write to the production application (the mirror destination is NOT in the prod-deploy WIF binding per architecture §5.4), or access to the §5.9 high-sensitivity tier. The Task 7 ADR MUST preserve all three points. **Trustee-administrative-control attestation is required** per AR-67: the mirror destination MUST NOT be administered by Solo Builder alone (that would defeat the bus-factor mitigation); ≥1 trustee admin minimum, ≥2 recommended.
8. The framework is **ready** for Tasks 7–11 (`_AWAITING EXTERNAL ACTION_`); Tasks 7–11 close per the AC text in the Story 0.3 file with provisional-vs-full closure language precision per [[feedback_closure_language_precision]]. AC-1 full closure requires Tasks 7–10 + Story 0.6 closure for the non-Solo-Builder restoration drill executor. AC-2 full closure requires Tasks 7–9 + Task 11 + Story 0.6 closure for the bus-factor exercise executor. Provisional closure paths exist for both ACs via the Story 0.1 AC-4 substitute-engineer model.

**Open follow-ups:**
- Trustee Panel + Solo Builder selection of mirror destination (recorded as a future `[CONTINUITY]` entry that supersedes this entry's pending status on Decision 5).
- Trustee Panel + Solo Builder selection of mirror credential model (supersedes Decision 6).
- ADR directory scaffolding scope decision (inherited from Story 0.2 Decision 002 Open Follow-up #6 — Solo Builder + Trustee Panel agreement required; Story 0.3 Task 7 may scaffold OR defer to Story 0.15).
- Code-escrow-mirror-destination ADR authored (replaces `[deferred ADR — placeholder procedure]` tags in `docs/escrow/code-escrow/mirror-procedure.md` and the workflow YAML's destination-configuration step comment).
- Mirror destination provisioning at the chosen platform with trustee-administrative-control attestation populated in `docs/escrow/code-escrow/mirror-destination-inventory.md`.
- GitHub Actions secret wiring (`MIRROR_PUSH_CREDENTIAL` + `MIRROR_DESTINATION_URL`) per Task 8; recorded in `code-escrow-ledger.md` "Workflow-secret-wiring log."
- Story 0.2 credential-inventory row addition for the mirror-push credential per Task 8 (envelope_class `prod-credential`; owning Story 0.3; re-seal trigger per §5.9 rotation cadence).
- First successful mirror-workflow run + ≥2-trustee read-access verification per Task 9.
- Trustee Panel review and ≥2-trustee sign-off on the framework-commit itself (the framework-commit row in `docs/escrow/code-escrow/code-escrow-ledger.md` is `Author-committed; awaiting trustee sign-off`; sign-off flips to `Trustee-ratified`).
- Restoration drill execution (Task 10): backup engineer per Story 0.6 (preferred) OR trustee-authorized substitute per the Story 0.1 AC-4 model.
- Bus-factor switch-to-mirror exercise (Task 11): non-Solo-Builder executor under bus-factor silence; provisional closure via substitute path; full closure requires Story 0.6.
- Story 1.1 release-branch model amendment trigger: when Story 1.1 commits the canonical branching model, update `code-escrow-mirror.yml` `on: push: branches:` list under a new `[CONTINUITY]` entry.
- Workflow-failure paging integration (Story 0.3 Open Question #4): Cloud Monitoring per architecture §5.6 + §5.10 backup alert path is NOT wired to GitHub Actions workflow-failure events at v0.3 time; flag for Story 5.x or Story 1.16x backlog. Silent mirror-workflow failure is a real risk; the AC-1 10-minute SLA cannot be enforced without active monitoring.
- Mirror destination branch-protection design (Story 0.3 Open Question #6): force-push semantics at the mirror are intentional (the mirror is a faithful copy) but mean a malicious force-push at the primary destroys mirror history. The destination's branch protection is an ADR decision interacting with `git push --mirror` semantics.
- **30-day takeover joint-discharge condition** (per AC-2 explicit text: "AC-2 alone does not discharge the 30-day takeover property — record this dependency explicitly in the ledger when closing"): the AR-67 + PRD §9.1.1 30-day-takeover property is jointly discharged by AC-1 (code accessibility + restoration drill verified) + AC-2 (continuity-of-development under bus-factor) + Story 0.5 (KT pack the external engineer reads to come up to speed) + Story 0.6 (the contracted external engineer subject of the 30-day takeover). A closure of any one of those four MUST NOT be conflated with the joint discharge. The condition is anchored in `docs/escrow/code-escrow/code-escrow-ledger.md` Bus-factor switch-to-mirror log section header for future closure rows; the ledger anchor is the operational reminder, this follow-up is the durable decision-log commitment. Closure path: when AC-2 closes (provisional or full), the closing entry in the ledger cites AC-1 + Story 0.5 + Story 0.6 status; when all four close, a follow-up `.decision-log.md` entry records the joint-discharge achievement.

### Decision 2026-05-29-002: Credential escrow framework scaffolding committed; sealing-mechanism + custodial-location selection pending

**Decision type:** Story 0.2 — Credential Escrow
**Status:** Author-committed; awaiting trustee sign-off
**Author:** Solo Builder (BigDev)
**Ratifying trustees:** _pending_

**Context:**
Story 0.2 of Epic 0 commits the credential-escrow framework per AR-67 + PRD §9.1.1 (the seven credential domains: prod DB access, Cloudflare admin, Dokploy admin, partner integrations, payment intent / banking, DigiLocker integration, DPDPA breach-reporting tooling). This entry records the framework scaffolding step (Story 0.2 Tasks 1-5, author-committable). The Trustee Panel + Solo Builder selection of sealing mechanism + custodial location (Tasks 6-9) is `_AWAITING EXTERNAL ACTION_` and will be recorded as a separate `.decision-log.md` entry that supersedes the "pending" status on the relevant fields of this entry per the Story 0.1 schema (supersession is recorded as a new entry referencing the prior; the prior is not modified).

**Decision:**
1. `docs/escrow/` is the canonical location for credential-escrow governance instruments per architecture §Workspace Layout (line 4172).
2. The five framework documents — `README.md`, `_envelope-template.md`, `credential-inventory.md`, `escrow-ledger.md`, `sealing-procedure.md` — are the governance instruments for credential-escrow authority. The sealing-procedure uses the same five-section template that Story 0.1 established in `docs/runbooks/`; the escrow-ledger mirrors the operational-readiness-ledger pattern from Story 0.1.
3. **Envelope-class separation per architecture §2.10a is a load-bearing structural invariant.** Every envelope carries an `envelope_class` field (`prod-credential` | `audit-mirror-credential` | `high-sensitivity-tier-credential`); the sealing-procedure's Prerequisites §1 enforces the assignment against `credential-inventory.md`; a sealing operation that violates separation MUST be aborted. The mechanism for structural distinctness (disjoint trustee subsets; separate sealing mechanisms; separate custodial paths; hybrid) is deferred to the credential-escrow-mechanism ADR.
4. **Sealing mechanism is not yet selected.** It is ADR territory and a future Trustee Panel + Solo Builder decision. Candidate mechanisms (informational; the Trustee Panel decision is the authority) are listed in the Story 0.2 file Task 6 and in `README.md` "Audit independence invariant — envelope-class separation". The sealing-procedure runbook tags every mechanism-specific step `[deferred ADR — placeholder procedure]` until the ADR lands.
5. **Custodial location is not yet selected.** Same status as #4.
6. **`docs/adr/` directory is not yet scaffolded.** Whether Story 0.2 Task 6 scaffolds it as a side-effect (analogous to how Story 0.1 scaffolded `.decision-log.md`), or scaffolding is deferred to Story 0.15 / a dedicated bootstrap story, is an open follow-up requiring Solo Builder + Trustee Panel agreement.
7. The credential-inventory enumerates 19 rows across the seven PRD §9.1.1 domains plus seven architecturally-adjacent envelope classes (KEK roots, HMAC tier-2 roots, audit-mirror write + read service accounts, partner JWT signing keys, telephony recording-storage, backup engineer access, DR runbook PDF custody). Per-row availability status uses three states with closure-language precision per `[[feedback_closure_language_precision]]`: `sealable-now`, `pending-system-availability`, `deferred-with-ADR`. At Story 0.2 closure time: 0 rows sealed; ~1-3 `sealable-now` (Cloudflare admin definite; Dokploy admin + trust bank operational pending Solo Builder confirmation during Task 7); 14 `pending-system-availability`; 2 `deferred-with-ADR` (partner credentials + partner JWT signing keys).
8. The framework is **ready** for Tasks 6-9 (`_AWAITING EXTERNAL ACTION_`); Tasks 6-9 close per the AC text in the Story 0.2 file with provisional-vs-full closure language precision per `[[feedback_closure_language_precision]]`.

**Open follow-ups:**
- Trustee Panel + Solo Builder selection of sealing mechanism (recorded as a future `[CONTINUITY]` entry that supersedes this entry's pending status on item 4).
- Trustee Panel + Solo Builder selection of custodial location (supersedes item 5).
- ADR directory scaffolding scope decision (item 6 — Solo Builder + Trustee Panel agreement required).
- Credential-escrow-mechanism ADR authored (replaces `[deferred ADR — placeholder procedure]` tags in `docs/escrow/sealing-procedure.md`).
- Trustee Panel review and ≥2-trustee sign-off on the framework commitment itself (the framework-commit row in `docs/escrow/escrow-ledger.md` is `Author-committed; awaiting trustee sign-off`; sign-off flips to `Trustee-ratified`).
- Initial sealing of `sealable-now` envelopes (Story 0.2 Task 7).
- Dry-run quorum-open + re-seal on a non-load-bearing credential (Story 0.2 Task 8).
- Bus-factor table-top execution scoped to then-sealed envelopes (Story 0.2 Task 9); full closure deferred to inventory completion across Stories 1.2, 1.5, 1.10, 1.13, 1.15, 3.3b, 7.7, 9.2, 12.x, 14.3, 0.6, 0.4.
- Story 0.3 (Code Escrow Auto-Mirror) closure → mirror coverage record update in `docs/escrow/escrow-ledger.md` (analogous to the same update committed for `docs/runbooks/operational-readiness-ledger.md`).
- Trustee headcount + disjointness definition: per Story 0.2 code-review Decision 5, the specific definition of "disjoint" (strict no-overlap requiring ≥6 trustees for 2-of-3 + 2-of-3; constrained-overlap with rules requiring ≥4; or hybrid) is deferred to the credential-escrow-mechanism ADR rather than committed in framework documents. Confirm the Trustee Panel headcount supports the chosen mechanism when the ADR is authored.
- **`[GOV]` schema-amendment entry: add "Story 0.2 — Credential Escrow" to the decision-type index.** Per Story 0.2 code-review Decision 6, the index at lines 9-16 above does NOT enumerate Story 0.2, but the entry above uses `Decision type: Story 0.2 — Credential Escrow`. Per Story 0.1's load-bearing schema discipline ("if a future story discovers the template is insufficient … the change is itself a `.decision-log.md` entry of type `[GOV]` proposing the schema amendment — not a silent rewrite"), this follow-up commits to authoring a future `[GOV]` entry that proposes the index amendment. The amendment is NOT performed in this entry to honor Story 0.2's scope-discipline commitment ("do not modify the decision-type index"); the `[GOV]` entry is the precedent-following path.

### Decision 2026-05-29-001: Operational runbooks scaffolding committed; trustee sign-off pending

**Decision type:** Story 0.1 — Operational Runbooks (scaffolding)
**Status:** Author-committed; awaiting trustee sign-off
**Author:** Solo Builder (BigDev)
**Ratifying trustees:** _pending_

**Context:**
Story 0.1 of Epic 0 commits the seven Phase-0 operational runbooks per PRD §9.1.1 (deploy, rollback, secret rotation, audit-log integrity verification, reconciliation manual-intervention, RBAC seed reset, multi-Pariwar provisioning), governed by the operational-readiness ledger and the five-section runbook template. This entry records the author-commit step; trustee sign-offs are recorded separately in `docs/runbooks/operational-readiness-ledger.md`.

**Decision:**
1. `docs/runbooks/` is the canonical location for operational runbooks per architecture §5.15.
2. The five-section template (`_template.md`) and the operational-readiness ledger (`operational-readiness-ledger.md`) are the governance instruments for runbook authority.
3. The seven Phase-0 runbooks are committed at the SHAs recorded in the ledger's "To-be-signed inventory" table.
4. The §5.15 broader runbook inventory beyond the seven Phase-0 topics is owned by other stories (see README "Related runbooks owned by other stories"); Story 0.1 does not silently expand to cover them.
5. AC-4 execution-path selection (sequence with Story 0.6 vs table-top substitute) is **not yet selected**. The selection will be recorded as a separate decision entry when the Trustee Panel ratifies the path.

**Open follow-ups:**
- Trustee Panel review and ≥2-trustee sign-off per runbook (Story 0.1 AC-2).
- Trustee Panel selection of AC-4 execution path (Story 0.1 Dev Notes "Cross-Story Dependency Note").
- Story 0.3 (Code Escrow Auto-Mirror) closure → mirror coverage record update in the ledger.
